Kitab-i-Badi (The Wondrous Book)

BH00004

Translated on 9 Qudrat, 183 BE using GPT 6.1 Sol

1 He is God, whose help is sought.

2 Your letter, conveying certain unverified accounts and untrue reports, reached this transitory servant. In one respect it opened the gates of joy, and in another it opened the gates of sorrow. As for joy, it gave news of your well-being; as for sorrow, it became apparent that, in the end, souls who associate partners with God have kept you from the realm of divine unity and deprived you of the breezes of divine love. We ask God to turn you away from them and direct you toward His radiant face.

3 When this servant observed that what you had written had not arisen from your own nature, but consisted of the lying words of souls who associate partners with God, which they had recounted to you, it became necessary, solely for the sake of God and out of love for you, to give a reply. Perhaps, through the power of the Lord of Lords, you may rend the greatest veil and the veils of glory with the fingers of power and detachment, ascend to an abode sanctified beyond mention, allusion, words, and expressions, and distinguish those who swim in the ocean of majesty and soar in the heaven of divine glory from the birds of darkness.

4 Although it is apparent that the greatest veil has entirely deprived your sight of the greatest vision, since you had offered words that outwardly took the form of counsel, it became necessary for this servant to inform you, to the extent of what I have received, of the surging ocean of divine favor and the manifestations of the radiance of everlasting glory. Perhaps you may not be deprived of the sanctuary of sanctified glory or left without a share in the sacred Kaaba of detachment.

5 Although words were heard from the blessed tongue whose import has caused this servant to lose hope in most people, this is nevertheless set forth solely for the sake of God, so that perhaps you may receive a breeze from the Paradise of sanctified glory and be granted a fruit from the tree that is neither of the East nor of the West. These are the words that were spoken: “Most people today suckle at the breast of heedlessness and ignorance. The days of their weaning have not yet come, much less their maturity. Therefore the Word has no effect upon them, for they neither understand nor perceive. Behold these people as sheep whom a child leads wherever he wishes. Thus was it revealed before, yet the people do not recognize it.”

6 Since numerous points were mentioned in your letter, it became necessary for this servant to write down each of your points and then give a reply, so that perhaps, in these days when darkness has encompassed the whole earth, some of those who seek and aspire may be guided by the light of the sun of guidance and not be left without a share of the Kawthar of life. He indeed guides whomever He wills, and He has power over all things.

7 This is the first point you wrote: “My hope in God, the Most Exalted, is that He may grant us a meeting, so that perhaps the dark limitations and veils of illusion may be removed, and those who swim in the sea of divine unity and soar in the heights may be distinguished from those who dwell in the pit of darkness and remain in the desert of bewilderment.” End of quotation.

8 As for your wish that a meeting take place and that through it the veils be removed, not every meeting has been or will be a cause of unveiling. In most cases, a meeting adds to the veil. Thus, a person who was a source of malice and a mine of corruption came, and far from your rending his veils, he added to yours. Had you been capable of rending a veil, you would certainly have removed the obstructing veils from that veiled soul. Nevertheless, God willing, we hope through divine grace that a meeting may take place in joy and delight, that we may all gather in the assembly of holiness and, freed from verbal allusions, find our way into the limitless worlds of meaning. For these days are days of unveiling and witnessing, not days of investigating conjectures and imaginings. From God we seek reunion and meeting; He is indeed the best Protector and the best to answer. It is clear that these words were not your own: you wrote them at the prompting of souls far removed.

9 A self that has received nothing from the Giver of being—how can it become a giver of being?

10 The likes of those withered, deprived souls have not distinguished right from left. How have they presumed themselves to be guides and saviors? The distinction between the breezes of divine souls and the odors of souls who associate partners with God is abundantly clear and evident. Not everyone with a human form is counted as human, nor is everyone who utters two words reckoned among the people of utterance. Limited souls have been and will remain deprived of the realm of divine unity, and diseased eyes have no share in beholding the rays of the sun of meanings. It is astonishing that you have taken the essence of illusion for certainty itself, and described what is imagined as something known. They were created from illusion, originated from it, and returned to it. Today, those who soar in the heaven of nearness to God and swim in the ocean of the All-Merciful’s mercy are radiant and shining like the sun. By God, if veiled souls cleanse their eyes of the disease of self and desire, they will at once receive the radiance of the Luminary of the horizons.

11 But what can I say, when the matter has become so confused for you? This did not come from you, but from one who had no mention in God’s presence and was deprived of the breezes of the days of God, the All-Protecting, the Self-Subsisting. Your words: “Yes, although outwardly my condition is good and I am passing my days in perfect health, I am deeply dispirited: first, from being deprived of the blessing of serving the friends, and second, because however much we struggle and strive, running from city to city and valley to valley in the hope that somehow we may find peace of mind, it does not happen, and they do not allow it.” This is astonishing on your part, and this servant is greatly bewildered by these expressions you have written: that you run from city to city to find peace of mind, yet do not find it. For these words suggest that, God forbid, you have failed to realize that roaming from city to city has never been and will never be a cause of peace of mind. If only you had remained in that same place and drawn a single breath purely for God! That breath would certainly have reached the hidden abode of the All-Merciful. For if the purpose of your journey was recognition of the Truth, you should come to this city, purified of all allusions and indications, and then meet and discern with your own eyes, distinguishing the Truth from all else—not become so veiled by the words of a soul rejected in God’s sight that you deprive yourself altogether of the beauty of the Desired One. The wayfarer will never reach the homeland of safety and security except with hands empty of all that is in the contingent world.

12 His words: “It had been intended that we should gain some comfort, be safe from the evil of strife, and find some respite. The whisperings of devils, following selfish desire, have stirred up mischief, undermined beliefs, and raised a new disturbance. Strife is asleep; may God curse whoever awakens it.” End of quotation. The heaven is almost rent [corrupted original text] by this statement, the earth split apart, the mountains crushed, the seas deprived of their waves, and the trees of their fruits. By God, the very pillars of existence, unseen and visible, are almost brought to nothing. What has issued from your mouth has turned pale the faces of those brought near to God. Anyone possessed of understanding would surely know that the face of the Primal Point has now grown pale from the blows of these words. He therefore laments in the companionship of the Most High, and with Him lament the angels who are brought near. Have you considered at all what you have written, what is meant by this statement, and whom it concerns? Yet it is not the character of the people of God to speak beyond their station. Whoever has not known his own limits and station has not known his Lord.

13 In any case, these servants, who have been nurtured by the hands of divine education and dwell in the shade of the tree of courtesy, have never wished, nor do they now wish, to answer such words from one like you. We have entrusted all matters to God; He is indeed the best Trustee. Listen to the call of a servant who offers sincere counsel: do not mistake the greatest grace for wrath, count the greatest peace as strife, or regard God’s all-embracing mercy as vengeance. The outward eye cannot distinguish sweet, refreshing water from bitter salt water; this has always required, and will always require, the sense of taste. If the sense of taste is altered by diseases that afflict it, it too will be incapable of perception. We ask God to bestow a delicate, sanctified sense of taste, so that we may distinguish the Kawthar of divine utterance from the bitter salt water of the self. The matter is in His hand. He is wise in all things and has power over all things.

14 If you desire rest, listen to this utterance of God: “Purify yourself of the defilement of self and desire, so that you may attain imperishable rest.” This rest lies in the shade of God, not elsewhere, for today everything outside His shade is reckoned a barren wilderness in God’s sight. God willing, We hope that you will cleanse your breast of the words and insinuations of those who associate partners with God, so that it may become the seat of the revelation of the Sovereign of Oneness and find complete rest from disputation, conflict, and contention.

15 You had written, “May we be safe from the evil of strife.” It would appear that you have regarded all the Manifestations of God who have appeared in the world, from Adam to the Seal, as strife, and have believed that speaking the word of truth is strife. Yet there is a vast difference between this strife and that strife, and none comprehend it except those possessed of insight. The strife of God is a strife from which a hundred thousand streams of peace and security flow. By God, if anyone possessed of a sense of smell were found, he would inhale the fragrances of Oneness from this strife. Elsewhere He says—His words, exalted be His majesty: “Alif. Lam. Mim. Do people suppose that they will be left to say, ‘We believe,’ without being put to the test?”

16 If you have deemed what appears from God to be strife, may a hundred thousand lives be sacrificed for such strife, which has distinguished and separated the idolatrous, Pharaoh-like souls from the Manifestations of Oneness. O servant, I ask God to bestow keen sight upon all people, to grant them pure souls, and to endow them with attentive ears, so that they may distinguish idolatrous words from divine words.

17 I can never consent to your writing such words. It had been stated that “the whisperings of devils, following selfish desire, have stirred up mischief and undermined beliefs.” My brother, do not call the holy breezes of the Lord of the worlds the whisperings of devils. By God, everything weeps, and every atom curses the one who instructed you in what has issued from your pen. Yet he is unaware within himself, for God has laid a veil of fire over his heart. He says what he does not understand and has spoken words that none of the manifestations of negation have spoken; he is indeed in grievous loss. Existence itself almost melts away at this statement, yet the one who prompted you is wrapped in a thick veil. As for the undermining of beliefs that you mentioned, know that no one’s beliefs have been undermined by the revelation of God and His Cause. Rather, they have become firm, steadfast, and more deeply rooted, except in the case of those souls whose very beings were of Sijjin in the previous revelation, but who, through their outward acknowledgment of that revelation, were reckoned as belonging to Illiyyin. When the greatest test appeared, those souls returned to their origin, while the radiant, holy souls reached their original homeland, which is acknowledgment and recognition of the Manifestation Himself. Blessed are those who have arrived.

18 God’s chosen ones and His loved ones are sanctified, purified, and free from those beliefs that depend upon worship of the calf. Any soul who, God forbid, remains veiled in this revelation of the Luminary of Meanings has never possessed, nor will ever possess, a belief that could be undermined. Have you not heard the song of the divine Dove, who says that if a soul were to remember and worship God, standing, sitting, and bowing down, for as long as the earthly and heavenly kingdoms endure, yet were to hesitate for less than an instant at the time of His revelation, his deeds would surely come to nothing? For the duration of that hesitation he has been, and will remain, in the fire—how much more so in the case of rejection and arrogance. We ask God to protect us from the whisperings of devils who have openly disbelieved in God and imagine themselves to be among those who know. No, by the Lord of the worlds!

19 You had spoken of a new disturbance. No new cause has been established or appeared. From the very first day that we, these servants, attained recognition of God, we have had our eyes fixed upon this holy, eternal Revelation, and have counted everything other than this universal divine Revelation as nonexistent in His presence. Indeed, some used to say that He referred to another out of expediency. Later it became clear that what had been said was the truth, and beyond it there is nothing but error. It is astonishing that you should have spoken in this way.

20 If by strife you meant a new outbreak of disorder, by God, besides whom there is no god, the very person who prompted you to write these words has stirred it up, as is evident. They had been together for forty years, and you yourself are certain of what has been said. On this journey, when that idolater entered among the people of God, he committed what Nimrod and Shaddad had not committed. Even so, We have not understood what has caused you, through his deception, to abandon your own sight, hearing, and heart, and to accept whatever he has said as certain.

21 We ask God to aid you in His Cause, to grant you recognition of the Manifestation of His own Self, and to purify you of the insinuations of those who turn away. What appears from God is mercy for those who acknowledge His oneness and chastisement for those who associate partners with Him. We ask Him to grant you a cup of the water of life that has flowed at the heart of contingent existence, so that through it you may detach yourself from everything but Him and draw near to the seat where nothing is seen but the lights of the face of your Lord, the Most High, the Almighty. We ask God that you may not be counted among those servants who have always raised such objections against God. Thus the idolaters wrote in protest to Husayn ibn Ali—may the souls of all others be sacrificed for Him—“You have caused division in the unity of the community, stirred up strife in the religion of your grandfather, and introduced certain innovations.” These very words are recorded at length in the books. Consult them; there is no further need for this servant to speak. At the mention of the final passage, the pillars of all things almost cease to exist. Yet We have been patient, and remain patient through God’s power and might, and We have referred the judgment to God, the All-Powerful, the All-Protecting, the Self-Subsisting.

22 The strife you have imagined has never been God’s way. This Revelation has appeared specifically to bring about harmony, unity, and concord among the people of the earth, so that perhaps all the diverse nations may gather around a single faith, and all may voice the praise and oneness of the Lord. It has become clear that you have not distinguished the one who puts things right from the one who causes corruption. God willing, We hope, through divine grace, that if you have not recognized the manifestations of Illiyyin, you may at least recognize the manifestations of devils, the dawning places of Sijjin, and the workers of corruption.

23 God has always called people to unity and concord. Examine the revealed words, so that this may become clear to you. This statement did not originate with you, for We have supposed that you did not commit this sin on your own initiative, but through what was suggested to you by one who was as one of Us. When he committed what he committed, God expelled him from the court of His glory; therefore he arose in opposition. He has taken for himself a calf, a body that lows, and calls people to it in protest against the Manifestation of God’s own Self, and to establish leadership for himself and for it. Such is the matter, if you are among those who know.

24 These words, which have set the hearts of the chosen ones ablaze, have been and will remain devoid of meaning and spirit. Yet concealed within the sheath of these words, in place of meanings, are sharpened swords that secretly strike the body of the Cause at every moment. If only someone possessed of sight and discernment could be found to perceive what oppression has appeared today, and to what extent the oppressors have risen to harm the Cause of God. We ask God to send down from the clouds of His command that which will consign them to their abode in the lowest depths.

25 You had also written—your words: “For this reason I had no intention of writing a reply. I wished to remain silent and not speak of such matters. I no longer have the disposition for disputation. These two days of life are not worth spending entirely on argument, conflict, mischief, and forced interpretations at the bidding of the self.” End of quotation.

26 If only you had not written a reply, or, if you had, had written from yourself rather than the fabricated words of idolatrous souls. Certainly, remaining silent would have been a hundred thousand thousand times preferable to making such statements as you have made. Speech is good when it serves to proclaim and establish the Cause of God, not to dispute and wage war with Him, as is stated in the divine counsels.

27 The tongue was created to remember the All-Merciful; take care not to defile it with the words of the manifestations of Satan. The tongue is the dawning place of the lights of truthfulness; do not make it the rising place of words of falsehood. The tongue is the treasury of My utterance and praise; do not occupy it with mention of this person and that, for in its own station He designates it a sign. You had written, “These two days of life are worthy,” and I have made it inexhaustible, for it is capable of uttering whatever it may. As long as existence endures, speech will not be cut off from it, unless an illness intervenes between it and the praise of its Lord. From it the discerning knower perceives that divine knowledge has never had, nor will ever have, an end. The pearls of the utterance of the worlds are concealed within it, yet have not increased its bulk; rivers of meanings flow from it, yet it has not diminished by a single atom.

28 In any case, much could be said on this subject, but this servant has confined himself to this brief account, that perhaps you may purify your outward and inward tongue of the disease of self and desire and cause it to speak in praise of God. For today, one who truly speaks is one who arises to praise God; otherwise, he has been and will remain merely a crier.

29 You had written, “There is no disposition left for disputation; no one has risen to fight and quarrel.” It is astonishing that you yourselves practice what you complain of. The disputation of the whole world lies concealed and hidden in these words you have sent. It seems that not a particle of the sayings of the deniers and the idolaters has been left out of this letter of yours. Each soul’s book has been placed in his hand, yet through inattention he has not become aware of it, and they send it from land to land.

30 This servant is greatly astonished that you have borne these weighty words. If only they would return them to their source! Good or bad, they have befitted, and will befit, the one who uttered them. We ask the surpassing grace of God to sanctify you from those illusory indications. It is not worthy that all should pass in disputation, warfare, mischief, and forced interpretations for the sake of selfish desire. It is astonishing that detachment has come to be established by words and claims. Thus, a person who has committed every conceivable forbidden act—so that even now, in every land, a trace of that tree of opposition remains—has nevertheless been regarded as detached. Beyond this, a person who has manifested every indecency, and whose fire of greed burns so fiercely that he has run from here to Istanbul for a morsel of bread—this has actually been manifested by him—need only meet some of those given to delusions, utter one long sigh, shake his head once, and say two words professing renunciation of the world, to be reckoned the foremost of the detached.

31 The idolaters’ weapon of warfare and defense is talk of death and detachment from the world. Through delusion they have instilled this so effectively that it has become impressed upon your heart. Yet those who are detached have another breath and another fragrance. If colocynth claims to be sugar, those with a sense of taste will not accept it. Every word requires a deed. Those who have objected to God have always uttered words such as these. Thus, you have attributed worldliness to a sanctified Soul who has suffered such afflictions that—by God, besides whom there is no other god—these servants could scarcely have thought He would survive; yet a person who has spent his entire life in the utmost pleasure and ease you have called detached. Woe, then, to those who speak according to their own desires and do not understand.

32 Furthermore, I did not understand what you meant by the interpretations you mentioned. Those who interpret are those who speak without having seen and pass judgment without having attained. By the grace of God, what we have stated belongs to the unequivocal divine pronouncements and to the accepted tenets of you and the people of the Bayán. Indeed, the Bayán was revealed for no other purpose than to command everyone, explicitly and without interpretation, that if a Soul should appear with divine verses, no one should object to Him. So emphatically has this point been stressed that the faith and certitude of all have been made dependent upon their acknowledgment of the very Manifestation. Yet you have called this unequivocal divine statement ambiguous and a forced interpretation. If this principle were removed from the Bayán, by what proof could the faith of those who claim it be established? How evil, then, is what they have supposed within themselves.

33 How quickly they have distorted the words of God! Certain counterfeit, counterfeit figures have caused the fragrances of the All-Merciful to be cut off from the people of the world. The difference is that these servants speak from what they have seen, while you speak from imagination. God willing, we hope that you will distinguish desire from God. If you are fair, you yourself will testify that everything that has occurred has always run counter to selfish desires, for the self that commands evil has never relinquished its own comfort and ease, nor will it ever do so. A Soul has appeared with all the divine signs, has continually suffered at the hands of enemies, and speaks what is recorded in all the Tablets. Nevertheless, you have construed what has appeared from Him as self and desire. May a hundred thousand lives of those brought near be sacrificed for this desire, at whose wafting musky fragrances spread from the Paradise of oneness; through whose movement still souls are stirred and set in motion, longing to meet God; through whose stillness troubled hearts become calm and assured; and through whose heat the divine fire is kindled in the trees of human existence, voicing the call, “There is no god but Him.”

34 We hope that you will not regard divine acts and the manifestations of lordship as selfish desire, and that you will turn your gaze toward fairness.

35 You had also written, “You have been mistaken. My heart burns at this: after spending year upon year among seasoned mystics and, as ordinary people say, living with the [corrupted original text] of the people, one should still be mistaken about the matter.” End of quotation.

36 Know at least this much: doubt has no place in the station of certitude. If you say that this servant has been mistaken in recognizing the wondrous Manifestation, then come and witness. If you say that this mistake concerns the revealed verses, then come, hear, and see. If you say that it concerns deeds, the deeds of everyone who acts are dependent upon His command and contingent upon His permission. The station of true certitude is sanctified above the conjectures and imaginings of the peoples of the world and those who harbor malice. It is clear that these words did not originate with you, for it is not supposed that you would follow conjectures and imaginings to such a degree.

37 All these things that have occurred were mentioned beforehand in the revealed Tablets. Beyond this, in the first year after arriving in this land, the Most Glorious Beauty was residing in a house near the Murádíyyih, while these servants were in another house. One afternoon He came out of the inner quarters, and all the emigrant servants stood in His presence, including this servant, Ḥájí Mírzá Aḥmad, Siyyid Muḥammad, and others. He said, “Today a strange thing was witnessed.” These servants waited to hear what had happened. He remained silent for ten minutes or more, then turned to these servants and said, “Today at dawn, a bird was perched on one of the branches of the tree in the house, uttering these words: ‘Muḥammad has come, and calamity has come.’ And the bird repeated these words three times.”

38 None of these servants thought of the Muḥammad who was present. Siyyid Muḥammad himself considered such a thing impossible concerning himself. We servants began to offer interpretations. Among others, there were several people in the surrounding regions who bore this name. Eventually we became certain that one of them must come to this land and cause some disturbance. After two full years had passed, what came to pass came to pass. It then became clear who was meant by the Muḥammad mentioned. By God, by God, his deeds and actions are not fit to mention! By His own truth, if I have stated a word other than what occurred—what occurred is precisely what has been recounted. Nor should they think that this incident has been mentioned to display miracles. No, by the Lord of the worlds! For we are certain that those whose hearts harbor malice toward God have never become convinced by any proof or evidence, nor will they ever do so, as has been foretold: “Even if they see every sign, they will not believe in it.” The point, however, is that his circumstances were known in a Tablet in which He has left out not a particle of the deeds of all created beings.

39 This statement of yours greatly increased my astonishment, for a person who was rejected by this community, and who was never and is not now fit to be spoken to, has so confused the matter for you that you now wander in the wilderness of doubts and roam the deserts of conjecture. It would be well for you to cast off these borrowed wings, fly on spiritual wings in the skies of the glory of divine holiness, and enter the delightful expanses of the spirit. You, who for years counted yourself among the worldly-wise, have at last been so swept away and shaken by a single gust of the tempests of conjecture and imagination that it cannot be described. God alone knows where He will finally bring you to rest. Knowledge of it is with my Lord, and I am only a servant who turns to Him.

40 We hope that you will turn from the cords of imagination and conjecture to grasp the firm and mighty cord, and leave the house of doubt and supposition to enter the goodly city of steadfastness, certitude, and assurance. I swear by God that my heart has burned and still burns deeply for you, for after countless hardships you have become ensnared by the speech of the wilderness of illusion and desire. We ask God to deliver you, to give you to drink what will purify you of what you have heard, to sanctify you from all besides Him, and to establish you beneath the shadow of His loving care. He is indeed the most merciful of the merciful.

41 You had also written: “My heart burns because, owing to the long interval since the Prophet’s mission, people like us have clung to religions based on interpretation. For a time we consoled ourselves with pleasant imaginings, suffering bitter anguish, thinking we had escaped a religion of forced interpretations. Now, according to what you say—as people put it, ‘the first cup, and already the dregs’—we must enter a religion of interpretation once again. Before the unambiguous statements of the Bayán have gained currency or become manifest, we must cling to the tails of its ambiguous statements.” End of quotation.

42 This much has been clear: those who interpret according to their own desires, and those given to conjecture and delusion, have always remained settled and at ease in their own abode. Even if a hundred thousand explicit declarations were sent down, they would never receive so much as a drop from them. As can be seen, the ocean of explicit declaration is surging, while all are gathered in the pool of delusion and interpretation. Interpretation, however, has innumerable degrees: one interpretation accords with God’s good pleasure, while another is made in objection to God. The divine words have endless interpretations, but no one knows them except God. Today, according to the explicit text of the Point of the Bayán—may the souls of all besides Him be sacrificed for Him—it is forbidden for those sheltered beneath the Tree of the Bayán to interpret or expound a single letter of the words of God, for no one knows them except the Manifestation Himself. Such interpretation has never been the prerogative of created beings.

43 As for the interpretation you mentioned, it is the interpretations of those who associate partners with God, which have always been and will always be rejected. Today, for example, they have set aside explicit declarations and remain veiled from the Manifestation Himself, occupying themselves, through conjecture and delusion, with expounding and interpreting His earlier words. Woe to them and to what they do! The purpose of all interpretations, expositions, and meanings of words and verses has been to attain the knowledge of God. Now that they have become veiled from God Himself, all these return to their own station, while those souls are lodged in the lowest depths.

44 As for your having written, “We suffer bitter anguish, thinking that we had found relief and escaped a religion of forced interpretations”—no, by God, you have not suffered such anguish. Had you done so, you would surely have entered the most pure abode and distinguished the Most Great Vision from the place where the ox appears. It does seem, however, that certain human devils have intended to sow discord between you and this lowliest of creatures. Yet God bears witness that, had your words been directed solely at this servant, whatever they might have been, I would not have been offended. But the hearts of all the worlds have burned at the implications of your words and the explicit statements of your utterances, for in your suppositions they all refer to the seat of Him who—by God, besides whom there is no other god—even if those whom you have taken as your beloved instead of God were to fly for as long as God endures, they would never reach the air in which blow the breezes of one of His names, which were created by His word, much less His own Self, the All-Powerful, the Most High, the All-Wise. But God has sanctified the hem of His robe from their barking and grunting and from the touch of the hands of these associators of partners with God.

45 Listen to this servant’s words and keep away from those souls. By God, through them the air of every city is corrupted. They profess belief in ‘Alí of old because the verses of God, the Almighty, the Generous, were sent down to Him; yet when they were sent down once again, they disbelieved in them. Surely God’s curse rests upon the liars and the wrongdoers! Nothing but rejection and objection has been, or is, discernible in the scent of your words. It is therefore very difficult for this servant to write on this subject. Otherwise, if someone should truly be found who has developed doubts through the insinuations of those who associate partners with God, it is certainly desirable that he state his doubts. We servants have no occupation in this land, and we wait, should any soul be troubled by a doubt, to give an answer according to our station and ability and his station, so that perhaps, through divine loving-kindness, his heart may be purified of false doubts and delusive conjectures. All we expect of you is that you consider what is said with a purified vision.

46 Among the words of His Holiness the All-Glorious—may the souls of all besides Him be sacrificed for Him—is this utterance: “Whoever has not purified his heart, his inmost being, his sight, and his hearing of what he possesses cannot recognize what has been sent down in truth in the tablets of manifest glory.” We therefore hope that God will send one of the sweet breezes of Riḍván upon the people of the contingent world, to cleanse and sanctify them all from the dust of disobedience and turn them toward the holy presence of the All-Merciful. He is indeed near and responsive to whoever calls upon Him.

47 O my brother! These birds of the heaven of love for the Manifestation of Oneness have gathered grain from the sacred harvests of divine unity and have listened to spiritual melodies of holiness. They are hardly likely to fall into the snares of the worldly, or to be kept back and deprived of the Lord of the worlds by the insinuating words of those who turn away and associate partners with God. Those who seek this end will only increase their own regret; nothing but trouble and weariness has been or will be their portion. For, by the grace of God, we roam the gardens of revelation and are present in the gathering of direct vision. We have passed beyond delusion and imitation and have found repose in the abode of witnessing and beholding. We have had, and have, no concern with the world and its people, as our deeds bear witness to our faith. We have not run from door to door for a monthly allowance and a morsel of bread, nor sent a woman to the palace, nor fabricated lies against God. By God, besides whom there is no other god, if you were to learn of the lies they have told you in that land and the fabrications they have attributed to God, you would surely flee to the wilderness and detach yourself from everyone on earth, if you are fair-minded.

48 You also wrote, “The first cup, and already the dregs.” You have used this expression without knowing where it should be used. Throughout the Bayán, the Point of the Bayán—may the souls of all besides Him be sacrificed for Him—explicitly declares: “Do not let anything created between the heavens and the earth—whether revealed books, upright scriptures, wondrous verses, manifestations in creation, or manifestations of religious law—veil you from the Manifestation Himself.” And although He has taken from all the covenant of allegiance to this most unassailable, most holy Manifestation, saying—exalted be His mention—“Glory be to You, O God! Bear witness that through that Book I have taken from all things the covenant of allegiance to Him whom You will manifest, before the covenant of allegiance to Myself. You and those who believe in Your verses suffice as witnesses to Me. You, indeed, are sufficient for Me. In You have I placed My trust, and You have ever taken account of all things,” all have turned away from His Beauty and clung to His slayer.

49 By God, besides whom there is no other god, it was plain what they would inflict upon this Blessed Beauty. Consider how much lamentation, mourning, and yearning is concealed in this very passage of the Point of the Bayán—may the souls of all besides Him be sacrificed for Him. In taking the covenant of this blessed Manifestation, He called upon God and all His servants as witnesses, so that perhaps the people of the Bayán would not rebel when the All-Merciful Himself appeared, and would feel shame before what was sent down in the Bayán. Nevertheless, what can now be seen has come to pass. Scarcely any time has elapsed; the words of the Revealer of the Bayán still ring in everyone’s ears, and traces of the pure blood of that wronged Martyr still remain upon the earth. Yet they have inflicted upon His own Self, His Being, and His Spirit what no religious community has ever inflicted. Here is the place for you to say, “The first cup, and already the dregs.” Now,

50 consider how greatly you use expressions in contrary, conflicting, and inappropriate ways. Of this Person He says, “I took allegiance to Him before taking the covenant of allegiance to Myself.” Nevertheless, those among the people of the Bayán who turn away have counted the souls who turn toward this all-merciful Person among the proponents of interpretation. With praise, we bring our complaint and sorrow to God concerning what has befallen Him at the hands of His servants, who were created by His word, “Be,” and it is.

51 You had also stated: “Before the unambiguous statements of the Bayán have gained currency or become manifest, we must cling to the tails of its ambiguous statements.” No one possessed of reason and discernment would utter such words. I am astonished that you consented to state in your own name the words of those who associate partners with God. It has never been fitting for the people of God to transgress the bounds of courtesy. We servants therefore leave the answer to some of the passages you have written to God, for being wronged has ever been beloved of God.

52 As for what you mentioned concerning the unambiguous and the ambiguous, your statement suggests that the distinction between them was not clear to you. Otherwise, you would have been certain that the unambiguous passages of what was revealed in the Bayán have appeared in their fullest and most perfect form. A brief account is therefore given here. Know that every community has its own account and explanation of the unambiguous and the ambiguous, as recorded in the books; to explain this in detail would prolong the discussion. In the sight of the True One, however, unambiguous verses are those in which the divine intention can be understood from the verse itself, without requiring another proof or statement to establish it. Now judge fairly: in the primary reality, which is that verse? It is demonstrably clear to everyone possessed of insight that, in the primary reality, that greatest Verse, which has never needed and will never need anything other than itself to establish its truth, is the Manifestation Himself. For everything and everyone besides Him depends upon His command. None denies this but an ignorant outcast.

53 If you say that the unambiguous verses are the divine commandments revealed in the Tablets, there is no doubt of this: we are all certain of them, believe in them, and hope for His grace. These unambiguous passages are unambiguous by virtue of their relation to the Most Just of Judges. It is evident that if someone were to know all the unambiguous verses, yet hesitate for less than an instant to acknowledge that greatest Verse, none of that person’s deeds would bear fruit; they have been and will remain mere dust. He whose verses have been and will remain unambiguous says: “The whole Bayán is a leaf among the leaves of Paradise in the presence of that pure Essence of Being.” Elsewhere He says: “What has been revealed in the Bayán depends upon His command and is conditional upon His permission and His will.” Elsewhere, addressing the people of the Bayán, He says: “Do not let the Bayán and what has been revealed in it deprive you of the promised Manifestation.” Unambiguous statements such as these have been revealed beyond all reckoning. Nevertheless, they turn away from Him through whose will all the unambiguous passages have appeared and from one finger of whose will all the rivers of knowledge and utterance flow, while imagining that they have understood and comprehended the unambiguous passages.

54 Now open the eye of fairness and recognize the people of deception and the embodiments of the insidious whisperer—that is, those who have suggested such words to you. Indeed, there have always been people who have called themselves by the name of the True One, cast the helpless and weak into the wilderness of conjectures and delusions, and occupied themselves with leadership. This is not the first arrow to strike Us, nor the first spear to strike the Beloved of the worlds.

55 If you say that the unambiguous passages are the descriptions revealed in the Bayán specifically concerning certain people, first, you have not understood whom those descriptions refer to. Second, by His own true Self, He has described all things in the most exalted terms, to such an extent that He has referred all names and attributes to every single thing. This is the station of “You see no disparity in the creation of the All-Merciful.” All besides Him are His creation, and all share in these attributes, for in the Most Great Perspective all have been and will remain on a single plane.

56 As for the divine verses that outwardly concern certain people, their purpose was to encourage those souls. Beyond this, all refer to the sign of the True One’s self-revelation. So long as that sign of self-revelation is present in anything, it is described and mentioned by all names and attributes; once that sign is withdrawn, it is nonexistent. Reflect on this, that you may be among those who understand.

57 Even if we concede that certain descriptions were revealed specifically concerning the person you mentioned, have you not heard Him say: “Do not let the Letters of the Bayán veil you from the Manifestation Himself at the time of His appearance”? The Letters of the Bayán are the first souls to have been described by all attributes and designated by all names. This very statement of the Sovereign of Eternity is clear evidence that some of the Letters would be veiled, as can be seen: a person counts himself among the Letters and Mirrors, yet is cut off from the hem of holiness and clings to the cord of self and desire.

58 He likewise says that at the appearance of the Most Great Luminary in another cycle, the dwellers of Sinai become nonexistent and as nothing. Reflect a little, that perhaps you may recognize the loftiness of the Cause. Since the greatness of the Cause is such that He says the dwellers of Sinai become dead and as nothing, the Letters and Mirrors have never had, and do not have, any standing in that court except after their love. Elsewhere He also says—exalted and mighty are His words: “It is lawful for Him who will appear to reject the one above whom there is none on earth, for that one is a creature in His grasp.” End of quotation.

59 These utterances bring names and attributes into being, whereas those are the words of veiled and shackled souls. By God, the suns of unambiguous statements, clear verses, firmly established proofs, and perfect words have now shone forth. Nevertheless, you write that Mírzá Yaḥyá cannot be rejected, although the utmost extent of his station was that he would enter the presence of the All-Glorious Beauty and divine words would be imparted to him. By God, despite his deeds, no one has assailed him. He made himself an outcast by turning away from the True One, by whose word he was created. Thus has the matter been explained to you, that you may be among those who know.

60 Now reflect on which of the divine verses are unambiguous and which are ambiguous, and recognize those who have taken hold of the ambiguous passages, cast the unambiguous ones behind their backs, and become among those who associate partners with God. Here I wish to mention some of the unambiguous verses revealed from the heaven of the will of the All-Glorious—exalted be His grandeur. By God, they suffice for all created beings, so that every person of faith who has an atom of divine love in their heart may cling to them, rescue themselves from the conjectures of the people of the earth and the fabrications of the manifestations of Satan, and thereafter remain steadfast in faith and experience no wavering. These are some of the verses revealed in a Tablet to one of the loved ones concerning one of the wretched. He says—mighty be His glory: “If you see the name Há, remind him of the verses of your Lord, that perhaps he may turn toward Him through turning to whom one is saved, and through turning away from whom one perishes. Thus has the Spirit spoken at this time between the heavens and the earths.”

61 Say: “O servant, listen to My words. By God, the True One, you will find no counselor more sincere toward you than I. Do not linger on the path, but pass beyond it as the clouds pass. Thus We commanded you before and command you now. If you will not accept this from Me, We will command you to do so hereafter, and again thereafter, until you pass beyond it through My all-conquering, all-powerful, all-wise sovereignty.”

62 Tear through the veils of delusion by My remembrance and then My name, and leave behind you everything that holds you back. Then appear suddenly through the appearance of your Lord, and do not be among those who wait. Beware lest leadership keep you from the remembrance of your Lord. By God, the True One, even if everyone in the heavens and on earth were to bow down before you, it would not benefit you if you were not beneath My shadow. Your own spirit bears witness to this, if you are among those who listen. Do you wish to be among those whom leadership held back in the days when the heaven of the Cause was split apart and He came upon clouds, and who disbelieved in God, their Creator and Originator, until they pronounced a sentence of death against Him after He had come to them with a testimony from God and a mighty proof?

63 By God, O servant, My purpose has been only to purify you of everything unworthy of you. Whenever I fall silent concerning you, the tongue of God speaks through My tongue and commands Me to convey the message to you. My duty is only to convey it clearly. Reflect within yourself for less than an instant: have you heard of a revelation greater than this, or of verses greater than those revealed in truth? Then speak with pure truthfulness and be among those who have turned toward the Most Great Vision on the day when the faces of all created beings have been turned away.

64 If you fear on account of your faith, take this Tablet and keep it in the breast of your trust in God. When you enter the place of gathering on the day when all contingent beings are raised before the face of your Lord through His wondrous, unassailable breaths, and God asks you, “By what proof did you believe in this Revelation?” then bring out the Tablet and say, “By this revealed, blessed, ancient Book.” Then read what has been revealed in it before the face of your Lord, in that abode where you will behold all the Prophets and Messengers. By God, then the hands of all will stretch out toward you, and they will take the Tablet and place it upon their eyes, longing to meet Me and aflame with love for Me. They will find in it the fragrances of My mighty, unassailable holiness. Thus have We set forth the verses for you, that you may be assured within yourself and be among those who are at peace.

65 If God were to punish you for believing in His verses in this Revelation, by what proof would He punish those who did not believe in ‘Alí before this, and before Him in Muḥammad, the Messenger of God, and before Him in Jesus son of Mary, and before Him in the One who spoke with God, and before Him in the Friend of God, until the succession of Revelations reaches the First Wondrous One, who was created through the will of your Lord, the Powerful, the One who wills?

66 O My Name, the Cause is too manifest to be concealed and too clear to be veiled; it shines like the sun at the zenith of noon. If you free yourself from the veils, you will reach it sooner than your gaze can return to yourself. This is indeed the certain truth.

67 Listen to the words of Him who speaks the truth, and do not dispute God’s verses after they have been sent down, even if all people bid you do so. Do not deprive yourself of His grace, even if all humans and jinn would keep you from it. Examine your Lord’s Cause with your own eyes, then recognize Him with your own self and spirit, for another’s recognition is no proof for you, nor is the rejection of anyone else an argument against you. Beware of being veiled from Him: were you to remain veiled from Him for less than an instant, your deeds would come to nothing. Everything previously sent down in the scriptures of God, the King, the Revealer, the All-Powerful, bears witness to the truth of what I say. End of quotation.

68 These are the revealed words that have been cited. Now judge fairly, and distinguish the unambiguous words from what is otherwise. If a soul has a spark of the fire of divine love in his heart, he acts according to what has descended from the heaven of the divine Will, and finds himself detached from all who are in the heavens and on earth, firmly established upon the plane of tranquillity, assured of God’s Cause, and independent of all else besides Him. By God, the proof is complete, the blessing perfect, the sun shining, the verses descending, and the Revelation most manifest; yet you have entangled yourselves in the wilderness of investigation, conjectures, and allusions. We ask God to grant success to us and to you, and to bestow upon us what He has ordained from His presence. He is indeed the Almighty, the Generous.

69 And further, your statement: “Bravo! What a thing has happened! People still have objections to the very foundation—that is, they have not yet understood what the message was or what laws it contained. Now new arrangements and a religion of interpretation have been set up that will cause a thousand kinds of turmoil.” End of quotation.

70 I intend no discourtesy to you, but I call God to witness that no rational person has ever spoken in this way. For if it depended on people’s objections and what they possess, the True One would never be manifested. In every age and era when the Sun of divine Reality has risen and shone from the horizon of the divine Will, all have raised objections, turned away in the strongest rejection, and counted that embodiment of oneness among the people of turmoil and corruption. Yet it is evident to every discerning person that the purpose has never been, and will never be, anything but unity.

71 Moreover, these people’s objections have not been confined to this Revelation: they still object to the Revelation of the Messenger of God. If people’s objections were to be the deciding factor, we would have to deny all Revelations—God forbid! Furthermore, the laws of the Bayān have been established and made manifest through this Revelation, not through those deluded souls you have imagined. By God, they have not acted upon a single word of it. God forbid that their deeds should be mentioned: Satan flees from their deeds. Many matters have become confused and hidden from you. We ask God to open your eyes through His grace, rescue you from the darkness of self and desire, and bring you near to the Lote Tree of the Utmost Limit. He indeed has power over whatever He wills, and He watches over all things.

72 The True One has never heeded, and will never heed, people’s words, rejection, or objections. He does whatever He wills, despite those who have disbelieved in God and turned away. If you reflect even a little, all these objections of yours apply to the Primal Point—may the souls of all besides Him be sacrificed for Him—and likewise to all the Messengers before Him. Listen to My words, then seek forgiveness for what you have done, that God may forgive you and blot out your misdeeds. He is indeed forgiving and merciful.

73 Another statement by that hateful instigator:

74 “Therefore, it is better to reinterpret your own thinking than to reinterpret what is unambiguous.

75 It is astonishing that the matter has become confused for you. Do not seek ambiguity in an explicit matter.” End of quotation.

76 These servants have reflected for themselves and, through God’s grace, have emerged from the thickets of interpretation into the pleasant, spacious plain of explicit statement. The interpreters are those who abandon the manifest, exalted True One, carve idols of fancy with the axe of conjecture, and then devote themselves to their own handiwork, prostrating themselves and bowing before it. Yet they count themselves among the worshippers of the True One and regard themselves as detached from all but God.

77 Listen to this servant’s words, free your heart from vain imaginings, and turn your gaze toward the Lord of all people. A friend is one who, on seeing a friend fallen into the whirlpool of destruction, helps him to the extent of his strength and ability, in the hope of bringing about his deliverance and salvation. We therefore hope that you will hold fast to the cord of divine providence, escape the whirlpool of impurity and darkness, and enter beneath the shelter of the radiance of the divine dawn.

78 You have passed over and disregarded a Cause so explicit, now present and speaking the truth, and have opened before yourselves the door of interpretation, which has always been unsound and infirm. It has gone so far as to instill falsehood in you yourselves, yet you write it down and regard it as true. Among other things, a letter arrived from there whose sender and writer were unknown, since it bore neither seal nor name. Yet certain false, fabricated statements were recorded in that letter. It is clear that Satan inspired them, and that the forger, whose identity is unknown, wrote at his prompting. The writer himself knows this falsehood, and you know it too; nevertheless, you have regarded it as true, and through conjectures and vain imaginings you wish to assign the right of Him to whom the right belongs to someone else. Now consider who has sought ambiguity in an explicit matter. We ask God to open your eyes, assist you to recognize the Manifestation of His own Self, and make you one of those who attain their goal.

79 Another statement by that objector: “Yes, I was not among them, but I believe I am better informed than those who were there for years. Moreover, the general principles that serve as the standard are at hand.” End of quotation.

80 As for your claim to be informed: no, by God, no one is informed at all, neither you nor anyone else. By God, besides whom there is no other god, everything they have instilled in you has been, and will remain, contrary to the truth. Would that the person whom they have now taken as the True One apart from God were like even one of the people in the marketplace! It is astonishing that you have sought to confuse this servant. All your words, conjectures, interpretations, allusions, arguments, unambiguous and ambiguous statements, and claims to learning in this matter refer and lead back entirely to Sayyid Muḥammad of Iṣfahān. His excellence is perfectly clear to these servants, as it was before. How astonishing is your insight! How well you know people!

81 In any case, I hope that the True One will remove that corrupter from our midst. He has wrought corruption the like of which has not been seen from the beginning of creation until now. Of course, he too has claimed to be informed. Know at least this: by God, besides whom there is no other god, he has never had any knowledge of the matter; everything he has said is sheer falsehood and utter fabrication. This fleeting, four-day life is not worth spending on corruption, hostility, and the perpetration of such disgraceful deeds. It befitted you, after that corrupter arrived in that land, to counsel him with goodly exhortations and words of wisdom, not to strengthen him, become his mirror, and repeat his words. God willing, we hope that he will not eat the wheat of Rayy.

82 We praise God for having protected these servants from his malice and deceit. What more can this servant say? These are days when the deluded listen to the words of a gnat of the barren land, but do not listen to the Word of God. If they had any fairness, they would all weep tears of blood and lament in the wilderness, for they use the words of the Primal Point—may the souls of all besides Him be sacrificed for Him—to argue for the truth of someone other than Him, while striking His own Self with the sword.

83 Beyond all this, we wish to see by what testimony and proof they believe in the Primal Point. Bring them forth, if you are truthful. By the same testimony through which they believe with certitude in the Primal Point, we believe with certitude in the Manifestation Himself, on evidence greater still. The difference is this: when the divine touchstone and the balance of divine justice appeared, the believer in God’s oneness was distinguished from the one who joins partners with Him. Judge fairly: if we deny this Revelation, its manifestations, and its verses, with what face and on what grounds could we any longer claim to believe?

84 As for what you wrote, “Besides, the universal principles that constitute the balance are at hand”—grave indeed is this statement of yours, for this is a station that God has placed above all the worlds. The Manifestation Himself is God’s balance and His scales of justice. Truth turns with Him as the shadow turns with the sun. Thus was it revealed in the Bayán, if you are among those who understand. Your likeness is precisely that of someone who picks up a piece of stone, takes it to a person who possesses treasuries of jewels, and declares, “With this stone I intend to recognize and distinguish your jewels.”

85 The balance in people’s hands may serve to recognize others like themselves. God’s own Self is sanctified above it and has never been, nor ever will be, known through it. The divine balance has always been, and will always be, His own Self. Whoever has recognized Him has recognized the balance; whoever remains veiled has wasted his efforts and is among the losers.

86 Surely the balance you have mentioned is the very balance that was in the hands of the learned of the age when the Primal Point appeared—may the soul of all besides Him be sacrificed for Him—and likewise at the earlier appearance of the Messenger of God and, before Him, the appearance of Jesus son of Mary. All the learned and the leaders used the balance of their own selves to raise objections against those Suns of Truth, until at last they issued decrees for the killing of those sacred Temples. Indeed, God’s curse rests upon the wrongdoers.

87 O heedless sleeper! Know that the divine balance has always been, and will always be, His own Self and whatever appears from Him. All this is a bounty from Him, if you only knew. If someone truly looks to the Point of the Bayán and His counsels, even if all the peoples of the worlds should turn upon him with sharpened swords and cut every limb of his body to pieces, he would not hesitate over this Revelation. For everything He wrote, everything sent down from the heaven of His will, has been and will remain an explicit reference to this Most Great Revelation.

88 If you do not look to the Bayán, if you deny the divine proof that all the Prophets and Messengers have brought, and if you do not regard the verses of God—which have always been and will always remain a conclusive proof—as the balance, then bring forward what you possess, if you are truthful. You must certainly produce that balance and that proof by which you establish the truth of your own claim today. No, by God! You will never be able to do so, nor will you have the power. All affairs are in His grasp, and all things are in the hand of His might. Everything besides Him is a creature in His presence; to this every discerning person of insight bears witness.

89 If anyone today denies these divine expressions, these manifestations of lordship, and these revealed verses that have appeared in this most exalted, most holy Revelation and flow like pouring rain, he has denied God and all the Messengers. He is indeed among those who associate partners with God: this has been inscribed in the Mother of Tablets by the Pen of the Cause.

90 One melody from the melodies of the Dove of the Bayán concerning the balance is recounted especially for those who seek the sanctuary of the All-Merciful, so that they may attain an understanding of it, detach themselves entirely from satanic manifestations, and tear apart the veils of vain imaginings in such a way that the inhabitants of the Concourse on high will hear the sound of their rending, and they will see themselves above the entire assembly of the Bayán, consisting of those who have disbelieved and associated partners with God and who flee from branch to branch to instill doubt and misgiving in the hearts of those whom the breezes of the All-Merciful have turned from the left hand of conjecture to the right hand of certitude. His words—exalted be His remembrance, exalted be His praise, exalted be His word, and how great His bounty—are: “He is the balance, His command is the balance, His conditions are the balance, and His indications are the balance. Whatever is related to the balance is a balance.” End of quotation.

91 In short, the balance has been and will be the Manifestation Himself and whatever He brings forth from the ocean of bounty and generosity. What He does today, what He says, and what He commands have been and will be the balance for all, both before and after. Today, regard anyone you see standing outside this holy, all-merciful balance as false, and count him as rejected. Do not—God forbid—presume to assail the very source of the balance. Fear God, who created you from a drop of lowly water.

92 The words of other hostile persons: “First, let me address the central claim and issue. That same Báb whom you call the Most Exalted Lord established His own verses as the proof of His truth and demonstrated His truth through those verses. None of the believers denies this, and it is certain that you too cannot deny it. Indeed, your acceptance of the Báb, and that of others, was based on His verses.” End of quotation.

93 By Him in whose hand is my soul! No one with a sense of smell could perceive the fragrance of truth in these words you have written. What sorrow and regret that, in this spiritual springtime, you have not adorned yourself with a wondrous adornment or inhaled the sweet breezes of the Riḍván of inner meanings! Your words resemble those of the veiled—indeed, they are baser. By God! If you reflected on them, you would feel ashamed within yourself and erase them all. A person’s worth and station are revealed by his words. In truth, a word is the mirror of the self, if you are among those who understand.

94 With such unsound words, these embodiments of conjecture have always kept people from turning toward the divine Unity. At what a station is the sanctity of the Cause seen, and at what a station these veiled souls! Hear my words, then leave those things behind you and turn with your whole being toward God, so that oceans of inner meanings and utterance may flow from your heart in remembrance of your Lord, the All-Powerful, the Almighty, the Bestower.

95 Although the pen feels ashamed to set down a reply to such words, since a reply has been deemed necessary, I state that these very words of yours refute you. You have written a proof of the Cause of God without realizing it. Thus God causes to flow from the pen what its writer is unaware of. Woe, then, to the veiled, from the punishment of a momentous day!

96 O brother! A barren woman has been held in little esteem among all peoples because she has been deprived of physical offspring. By God! Those whose hearts have become barren of the emergence of the virginal truths of divine knowledge appear far more deprived. Now it can be seen that the hearts, inner understanding, and sight of most of the veiled have become so barren that they cannot comprehend what they themselves write, much less the allusions of the divine words and the gems of consummate heavenly wisdom. By God! These people are barren of God’s mercy, of truthfulness, and of fairness. Nothing has appeared from them but what intensifies God’s anger and wrath against them, yet they do not understand.

97 You wrote, “That same Báb whom you regard as the Most Exalted Lord.” From these words it is clear that you do not regard Him as the Most Exalted Lord, or else you have concealed your belief, like your guides, who in some places deny Him and disavow Him, while sending stacks upon stacks of fabricated books in every direction to establish the truth of their own claims. You, and all who are in the heavens and on earth, know that We have been certain, acknowledging, assenting, declaring, remembering, stating, proclaiming, crying aloud, calling out, affirming, speaking, conveying, and raising Our voice at its highest pitch that He is the Most Exalted Lord, the Lote-Tree of the Utmost Boundary, the Farthest Tree, the Kingdom on high, the Dominion of the Cloud, the Divinity of everlastingness, the Spirit of Glory, the Most Great Secret, the Most Perfect Word, the Manifestation of eternity, the Most Bountiful Temple, the finely adorned Mystery, the Lord of the nations, the surging Ocean, the Most Exalted Word, the Primal Pearl, the Hidden Scroll, the Treasured Book, the Beauty of Oneness, the Manifestation of the divine Identity, and the Dawning-Place of eternal Self-Sufficiency. Were it not for Him, existence would not have appeared, the Desired One would not have been known, and the beauty of the Worshipped One would not have emerged. By God! Through His name the heaven and all that is within it, and the earth and all who dwell upon it, were created. Through Him the seas were made to surge, the rivers flowed, and the trees bore fruit. Through Him the religions were established and the beauty of the All-Merciful appeared. By God! Were We to describe Him until the end that has no end, my heart’s thirst in its love of mentioning His names and attributes would not be stilled—how much less its thirst for His own sanctified, mighty, beautiful Self!

98 Be a witness and bear testimony: you have permission to show this very Tablet to any of the souls who have turned away, as you wish. Your guide obtained divine Tablets in the handwriting of the heavenly Branch and showed them to certain people, while himself disavowing them. This is what he did in secret. In public, however, he bows his head and exalts himself among the people of the Bayán, who have taken him as a lord for themselves. He offers them arguments to establish his own fabricated and false self, and rejoices in his leadership over them. Say: Woe to you, O fearful, treacherous, disappointed loser! Nothing will avail you today, even if you cling to everything created between the heavens and the earths.

99 Would that they had been content with this! Instead, they wrote words resembling those and showed them to certain people in the name of the True One. God knows what is in his secret heart and has recorded all things in a manifest register. Now you too, if you wish, may show this Tablet to any soul you choose. By God! My Lord has not protected Himself, nor will He ever protect Himself, and He awaits the cross from the Jews and the spear from the spear, if you are among those who understand. Know that the True One awaits what befell His previous Manifestation, and likewise what befell the Messenger of God before Him, and, before Him, the Spirit, and before Him, the Friend. He has feared no one and will never fear, through God’s grace and loving care. Gather, then, to shed His purified, mighty, inviolable blood.

100 As for your statement that He established verses as the criterion of His own truth and proved His own truth through verses: these very words, if you understood, have flowed from the Pen in proof of this Cause, but you have not grasped them. For are you certain of these verses you have mentioned, or not? If you are not, all these words you have written have been and will remain futile. If you are certain, why do you deny and turn away from these revealed verses, which descend at every moment like pouring rain? Know for certain that those endowed with sight and understanding will not give you the chance to proceed beyond your first statement. Through this single statement of yours, they establish and confirm God’s proof and His testimony against you. Just as that Tree of Oneness established His Cause and we came to recognize it, so now, in the same way, the Cause of God is manifest, the verses of God are revealed, and these servants have attained certainty. These servants have not been, nor will they be, among the deniers. The deniers are those souls who have believed in part of the Book and disbelieved in part. No, by God! Rather, they have disbelieved in all the Books. For today, if a soul turns away from a single letter of these verses, his rejection of them all is established and confirmed. We have believed in what was revealed before and what is revealed now, and all things bear witness to this, if you are among those who are certain. How well you reason! You are occupied with objections and disputes against the Beloved of the Primal Point, while writing that His truth was established through verses. You imagine that you look to the revelation, yet you are far indeed from its Revealer.

101 As for your statement, “It is certain that you too cannot deny”: our inmost being, our existence, our tongue, our heart, and our limbs bear witness to His verses, if you are among those who understand. I swear by the Sun of the glory of divine unity that a single letter of the words of that Manifestation of the names and attributes is dearer and more precious to the Most Glorious Beauty than all that is in the heavens and the earths. How much more is this true of these servants, who are as nothing before a single letter of His and are humble before all that has appeared from that Tree of Truth, that Sun of loving care, and that Moon of compassion. The difference between these servants and you is this: you profess certainty in words, from behind a thousand coverings and veils, while turning away from His Self, His being, His spirit, and His essence. Would that you had been content merely to turn away! No, by His own Self, the All-Merciful, the Ever-Merciful! Rather, night and day you are occupied with devising ways to shed His good and pure blood, as became clear from the plot contrived in that land. These servants, however, are certain of, assent to, and acknowledge His verses, His words, His Manifestation, His Self, and His spirit—all of them. Recognize, then, the condition of those who turn away, the station of God, the Most High, the Most Great, and the condition of these poor and lowly ones, and

102 You further wrote, “He himself will also distinguish verses from what are not verses better than others; indeed, he is the one who must make the true distinction. Certainly, such a person will not be a liar, nor will he act according to selfish desire.” End of quotation.

103 If you have written on the basis of conjecture and supposition, these servants proclaim with certainty between the heavens and the earth that there is no God but Him, that the Point of the Bayán is indeed His Balance and His Manifestation, that everything revealed by Him is the truth, beyond all doubt, and that He is indeed the One who distinguishes truth from falsehood and doubt from certainty. Now, concerning this One who distinguishes, whom you have mentioned: are you certain of and do you assent to all His verses, or only those you have interpreted according to your own self and desire and found to accord with them? If you are certain of all that has been revealed by Him, He says—exalted be His mention—“Everything in the Bayán is a gift from God for that Manifestation of self-subsisting glory.” And afterward He says, “Glory be to You, O God, my God! How insignificant is my mention and all that is attributed to me when I wish to ascribe it to You! Accept, then, through Your grace, me and all that is attributed to me. You are indeed the best of those who bestow grace.”

104 O heedless ones among the people of the Bayán! Despite this humility of the Primal Point before His other Manifestation, you have once again failed to take heed and have occupied yourselves with these assertions. By God! If you reflect even a little, you will lament for yourselves, for in this passage that blessed Being has spoken of Himself with utter humility and complete self-effacement. What place, then, is there to mention the station of the mirrors and what is attributed to them? Has a single line of the Bayán been seen that does not culminate in this most wondrous, incomparable remembrance? No, by His own Self, the True One! Yet you do not understand. By God! You have fallen into such a state that it is as though a thousand chains prevent you from moving. At last, step out for a moment from the wilderness of allusions and enter the delightful expanse of detachment, so that you may recognize the unassailable True One through His own sanctified Self and detach yourself from all besides Him.

105 On what evidence have you described that Countenance of Oneness as the One who distinguishes? If it was on the basis of verses, why, then, do you turn away from this One who distinguishes, who has appeared with verses before which everyone endowed with verses bows humbly in His presence, except those whom the winds of leadership move as they please? At one time they dash them against the mountaintops, at another against the wilderness of error; at one time they cast them into the depths of extinction, and at another leave them beneath the wolves of self and desire. Say: Die in your rage! By God! The One has appeared through whose word the proof of the first and the last was established, and through whom it will remain established until the end that has no end. None denies this but every sinful, inveterate disbeliever.

106 As for your statement, “He will not be a liar”: although you have outwardly affirmed His truthfulness, this affirmation was a mere semblance and has been taken up. For had you been certain of the truthfulness of the Primal Point, you would not have denied the Source of truthfulness, by an act of whose will the very reality of truthfulness is created and praised. Your affirmation of His truthfulness here was intended to enable you, through this outward affirmation, to deny His being and His reality. In the same way, the divines of the past, at the appearance of the Point of the Bayán—glorified be His majesty—said, “The Imams of guidance are certainly truthful. There are widely transmitted traditions from them stating that anyone who says the Qá’im must be born is a liar, a hypocrite, and a fabricator, and must be put to death.” They also argued that “Seal of the Prophets” is God’s word, explicitly revealed in the Qur’an, and that God is certainly truthful and His Prophet truthful. Then, in objection to God and the Manifestation of His own Self, they uttered these words: “The words of this claimant are false and devoid of credibility, for he has spoken contrary to the Book of God, His established ways, and His truthful Prophet. Thus, in his book it says that Prophets have come from the beginning that has no beginning and will come until the end that has no end.”

107 They raised objections in precisely the same way you have done; indeed, your objections are even more vehement, if you only understood. Now consider how deluded are those who have turned away and instilled these words into you. It is as though they have not attained a single letter of the meanings of the divine words or received a single draught from the ocean of self-subsisting glory. Woe to them and to what their selves and desires have commanded them! Whatever you may cite from the Bayán—even if we concede that you have understood it all, though this is impossible, for no one knows its meanings except God, and that you also carry out all its prescribed deeds—it will not benefit you at the time of the Manifestation unless you acknowledge that Manifestation. For He explicitly says, “Do not let what has been revealed in the Bayán veil you from that Countenance of the All-Merciful.” How much less, then, can these few words you have mentioned avail you!

108 Or, after the truth of this good and blessed word has been established, everything you have mentioned, or may afterward adduce as evidence, refers entirely to the limited selves of those who mention it and argue from it, while the True One is sanctified above all. It is abundantly clear that, had you regarded the Primal Point as truthful, you would never have denied this most wondrous, most unassailable Manifestation. Now, with your tongue you deem Him truthful, but through your actions—God forbid—a liar. Seek forgiveness, then, for what you have thought and said and written, and

109 Say: “Glory be to You, O God, my God! I am the one who neglected his duty toward You and followed the paths of conjecture and delusion, for I was veiled from recognizing the Manifestation of Your own Self. I adduced as evidence that which burned the liver of ʿAlí and then the heart of Muḥammad, the Messenger of God. It is as though I had slain all existence, unseen and seen. Look upon me, then, O my God, with the glances of Your compassion, and do not abandon me to my own self and desires. Then purify me of the allusions through which I was deprived of the gardens of union with You and of meeting You. You indeed have power over whatever You will, and You are indeed the All-Protecting, the Self-Subsisting.”

110 You also wrote: “Can anyone, whether an outsider or an insider, whether on the basis of common report or actual fact, doubt that the Báb confirmed Mírzá Yaḥyá—that is, that He wrote in various terms to the regions and the believers affirming his rightful standing: ‘After Me, it is he, and obedience to him is incumbent on everyone. Whatever the position of legatee and successor may be, after Me it is he’? He stated this explicitly and in plain terms for the followers of tradition and the common people. But for those endowed with insight and understanding He used other words: ‘The Cause will culminate in the name of the One, for His appearance is in itself a proof and requires no written designation.’” End of quotation.
