By God, you have committed what has burned the hearts of those brought near, and

398 If only you had regarded the True One as you would any person on earth, for you have never dared, nor do you now dare, to show such insolence or speak in this way to those people. By God, if you could hear, modesty itself disowns you, and the very being of courtesy seeks refuge in God from your deed. Courtesy is a human virtue, distinguishing a human being from what is beneath him. For any soul who has not attained it, nonexistence has assuredly been, and remains, preferable to existence. Yet toward that Seat for whom the very essence of courtesy was specifically created, you have acted in a way that everyone possessed of modesty is ashamed even to mention. The veil of your courtesy and modesty has been torn apart, you company of the ungodly!

399 That holy Essence says: “O people, I have come to you from the dawning-place of the Spirit with the tidings of God, the All-Protecting, the Self-Subsisting. I do not speak of My own accord; rather, your Lord, the All-Merciful, speaks through this truthful, eloquent, mighty and beloved tongue. O people, do not kill Me with the swords of hatred, nor pass against Me a judgment that violates the sanctity of God, the All-Powerful, the Almighty, the Beloved. O people, He calls to you through My tongue, speaks within My breast, and has raised Me up for His Cause. By God, I find in Myself neither movement nor stillness except at His command. Reflect, so that you may perceive. Does the heavenly river of life flow from turbid springs? No, by the Lord of the worlds, if only you knew!

400 O people, this is the wellspring of God’s Will, from which the rivers of His purpose have flowed in truth, as He has willed. He is indeed the One who rules in truth; He is indeed the Truth, the Knower of the unseen. If you find within yourselves any sense of taste, drink from it, that you may discover in it the sweetness of utterance and the fragrance of God, your Lord. This is the word of truth, and beyond truth there is nothing but error, if only you understood.

401 Do you object to the One who sent Me and speaks within My breast? Alas for you, then, and for what you do! Is it fitting for anyone to object to the One who has come to you with God’s verses and says that they have been sent down from Him? Be fair, then, if you are fair-minded. O people, I cannot draw a single breath except with His permission. You will find the breath of the All-Merciful coming from this Soul whom God has sent in truth, if you turn toward Me with your hearts.

402 Do not strip yourselves of the robe of courtesy and fairness, and do not utter what you have been forbidden in the tablets of your Lord, the All-Protecting, the Self-Subsisting. Whoever is deprived of courtesy is indeed naked, even if he wears all the silks of the earth. This has been sent down in truth in preserved tablets of glory. Whoever has no courtesy has no faith. To this, what has been revealed in the Bayán bears witness, if you would bear witness. Courtesy was created in the Bayán for this Beauty, as was every good name, if only you knew.

403 By the life of Him who has appeared in truth! In the Bayán He intended none but My own Self; He breathed only in remembrance of Me and ordained every good thing solely for My Beauty. Were it not for My remembrance, the Bayán would not have been revealed, nor would the Beauty of the All-Merciful have uttered a word among you, for nothing befits the likes of you but your own words, if only you understood.

404 The Bayán was revealed for My own Self, and I now recite it at all times. What is being revealed now is indeed the Bayán, if only you knew. What is being revealed now is to bear witness to what was revealed before, and what was revealed from Him is to serve as proof of what now descends from the Pen of God, the All-Powerful, the Almighty, the Beloved.

405 O people, I am His own Self, and He is My own Self. He sacrificed Himself for Me, and I have taken My life and My soul in My hands to expend them in His path, for His love and His good pleasure. Every atom bears witness to this, if only you could hear. Fear God and do not separate Me from Him, although God has placed this beyond your power. Were all who are in the heavens and on earth to unite to separate Us, they would have neither the power nor the ability.

406 He is now seated at the right of His throne, with His fingertips between His teeth, looking upon you in reflection on what has proceeded from you, you company of those who associate partners with God. He cries out and weeps over what has befallen Me and what will yet come from you. He now recounts to Me what is hidden in your breasts and weeps; at His weeping, the inhabitants of the Concourse on high lament. Yet you neither know nor perceive.”

407 Nevertheless, you have inflicted what has had no parallel on earth. If you had even a little awareness, you would realize that the objections you have raised are directed against God, for it is God who has sent down revelation and will continue to do so; no one else has had or has any part in it. Yet you will not perceive this, just as the objections raised by former religious communities were made without understanding. Had they been aware and realized that those objections were directed against God, they would never have occupied themselves with objecting. For this very reason, the manifestations of negation have always inflicted upon the Manifestations of affirmation what pen and tongue are ashamed to recount and describe.

408 Soon what He has foretold will come to pass, for you have not been content, nor will you be, with rejection and objection alone. The Ancient Beauty says: O deaf one of existence! I will recount one melody from the melodies of the Dove of My previous Revelation, that perhaps you may understand a little. Even if you do not understand, the realities of all things may listen, refrain from tearing the veil of modesty, and not cast the adornment of courtesy from their forms. It is this, addressed to the Letter of the Living and the Greater Vaḥīd—My glory rest upon them both—when they asked about this most unassailable, most holy Revelation. His words—exalted be His majesty—are: “By Him who is alone in might and majesty and brought things into being from nothing through the exemplar! Certitude is ashamed to attain certainty concerning Him or to bow before Him, and proof is ashamed to point Him out to another. Are You seen, that I might know You, or do You bear witness to anything other than Him, that I might infer Him from it?”

409 Of that Essence of eternity He says that certitude is ashamed to attain certainty concerning Him or to bow before Him, and proof is ashamed to serve as a proof of His very Self. Yet you, shameless and immodest, have arisen to undermine the Cause of God in a manner whose like is impossible in all the worlds of being. You have torn the veils of modesty and reverence with the fingers of association with God. We ask God to tear away your veil, expose your wicked self among the peoples of the world, strip you of the mantle of concealment, divest you of the garments of respectability, and disclose to His servants what you have been doing. He indeed answers those who call upon Him. By God, heedless one! Through your deed all things have been thrown into turmoil, the covering of the veil of purity has been torn, and the pillars of the exalted Concourse have been shaken.

410 I swear by the Beloved of all contingent being that henceforth you have shown and show regard for the sanctity of none of the Manifestations of God, but only for souls who associate partners with Him—and even that is because of the insinuations and conjectures concealed within those souls. Another utterance of the Revealer of the Bayān is now presented solely for the sake of the All-Merciful, so that perhaps a breeze from the paradise of divine words may pass over the inhabitants of the worlds of being. Perhaps, through its blessings, holy souls may emerge from behind their veils and coverings into the realm of visibility, attain what God desires, act upon what has previously been stated, and cherish these days as a precious opportunity. Likewise, perhaps those who resemble such souls may understand, at least to some degree, how highly the sanctity of this Revelation has been regarded in the sight of God, and may thus be restrained from insolence.

411 His words—exalted be His honor—are: “I have heard your letter, and what it contains is a gem. Were it not for what it contains, I would not have answered you on that sheet, nor then, O ʿAlī, on whatever has been ordained in creation. How momentous is the mention of Him about whom you have asked! This is higher, mightier, more glorious, more unassailable, and holier than hearts can attain through recognition, spirits through bowing before Him, souls through praising Him, or bodies through recounting His splendor. How great is your question, and how small your own being! Is a sun among the mirrors of His revelation in the Point of the Bayān to be asked about that Sun before whose countenance those suns will bow on the day of His revelation, if they are true suns? Otherwise, they are unworthy of the loftiness of its sanctity and the sublimity of its mention. Were you not of the First Unity, I would have imposed a penalty upon you, since you have asked about God, who created you, sustained you, caused you to die, and raised you up in this form of yours through the Point of the Bayān, in that revelation unique in existence. First, then, whenever you wish to bring His mention to mind, say nineteen times: ‘Glory be to Him who possesses the kingdoms of earth and heaven.’” He continues—exalted be His mention—until He says: “I have written a gem concerning Him, namely, that He is not to be consulted through My indication, nor through what has been mentioned in the Bayān. Indeed, by His might, that utterance is greater in the sight of God than the worship of all that is on earth, for the essence of all worship culminates in this. As you have known God, then, know the Manifestation of His Self, for He is too glorious and exalted to be known through anyone other than Himself or to seek counsel through the indication of His creatures. I am indeed the first servant to believe in Him and His verses, and from the pristine gardens of the paradise of His recognition I have taken the gardens of His words. Indeed, by His might, He is the Truth; there is no God but Him. All subsist through His command.”

412 Now, in light of these utterances of the Revealer of the Bayān—glorious is His grandeur—consider the standing of those mirrors who, apart from God, have assumed a right for themselves, and the standing of those who turn away and raise objections. Consider that Essence of glory whom the Beloved of the worlds, through one letter of whom all who are in the Bayān were created, mentions with such reverence that He says: “Were you not of the First Unity, I would have imposed a penalty upon you.” Reflect a little, then, on the purpose of this most severe warning to such a person. If you possess even a little understanding, these very words should cause you to cry out and raise the lament: “Alas for what we have neglected in our duty toward God!” Yet the questioner had written nothing contrary to courtesy and respect in his petition. Merely for asking, He warned him in this manner, so that he and the rest of the people of the Bayān might take heed and not ask anyone other than His own Self about Him. For He Himself has been His own proof and is not known through anyone other than Himself. Thus, at the end of this epistle, He says that whoever beholds that Ancient Beauty with any eye other than His own will never recognize Him.

413 His words—exalted be His mention—are: “Behold with the eye of your heart, and look upon Him only with His own eye, for whoever looks upon Him with His own eye perceives Him; otherwise, he remains veiled. If you desire God and attainment to His presence, desire Him and look upon Him.” Likewise, to honor His Self and manifest His station, He says elsewhere—glorious be His honor: “Indeed, He—glorious be His mention—makes His Self known to all things. I am ashamed to say that all things know His Self, in the same way that I have made all things known through My verses, for whatever you find among all things was created for Him. God is too glorious and exalted to be known through His creation; rather, creation is known through Him.” End of quotation.

414 Observe with what courtesy and honor He speaks in all these utterances concerning the subsequent Revelation. He says that He Himself makes His holy Self known to all things, and: “I am ashamed to say that all things know His Self.” If you were to reflect for less than a moment on these utterances of the Tongue of the All-Merciful and be nourished by the wholesome fruits of recognition concealed within them, you would shatter the idol of delusion, fashioned by the hands of conjecture, and turn toward the Eternal One. Yet it is most unlikely that you will receive this grace, for your insolence has exceeded all bounds.

415 By the Point of existence and the Beauty of the Adored One, this servant is bewildered by people such as you. Such are the words of the Most Exalted Lord, yet you speak of Mīrzā Jamāl and Mīrzā Kamāl in mockery of God! You have imagined that these words will undermine the reverence for God among the people, just as those who turn away from God have used these very words to fabricate slanders against the Beauty of the All-Merciful. Yet you are unaware that, in the eyes of everyone with insight, these very words you have uttered have made and will make you more contemptible, more wicked, and more remote than any description of evil can convey. Everyone of standing shuns the conduct of such souls who associate partners with God, and everyone of worth seeks refuge in God from the desecration you have committed.

416 Likewise, these utterances establish beyond doubt that none other than His holy Self has been or will be His proof, and that asking anyone other than Him in order to recognize His Self has always been and will remain unacceptable. Whoever wishes to recognize that Essence of the Unseen must recognize His own Self through what proceeds from Him, not through the words of those who came before or come after. By Him in whose hand is my soul! In the garden of these divine words the exalted roses of oneness are visible, and from the lote-trees of these written words the ripe fruits of spiritual knowledge are available. Yet no one has a share in them except those endowed with sight, smell, and taste. Today none are accounted among those endowed with sight, taste, and heart except the people of Bahāʾ, whose faces God has illumined with the lights of His beauty, whom He has sanctified from all besides Him, to whom He has made known that which has thrown hearts into turmoil, whom He has established in the shade of the Lote-Tree of His Cause, and whom He has detached from all the worlds.

417 Now, since the people of the Bayān travel contrary to God’s good pleasure, walk in the wilderness of delusion, and remain deprived of God’s intended One and His verses—as he has written, asking where in the Bayān this blessed, Most Great, Most Ancient, Most Glorious Name appears, and in what terms—this servant has therefore descended from the most distant abode and the Lote-Tree of the Utmost Limit, the station of “Recognize God through God,” and turned toward the realm of limitation, the station of mention and allusion. Perhaps they will accept what this servant states in this station and, holding to the cord of divine words, escape the perishing depths and walk into the everlasting realm of light.

418 As to his writing about whether the Bāb’s praises refer to a particular person or to the name Bahāʾ, first say: By what evidence did you acknowledge the Most Exalted One—may the spirit of all besides Him be sacrificed for Him—who called Himself the Remembrance and referred all the most beautiful names to His own blessed Self? By what proof did you become certain and assent? Look now to that same evidence and proof. All names and their kingdom have been and are created by the word of the Manifestation of Revelation, and all circle around Him, but you do not recognize this and are among the heedless. He says that wherever the most beautiful names are mentioned in the Bayān, the intended referent has always been and will always be the subsequent Revelation. Likewise, every mention of good revealed in the Bayān has, in its primary reality, referred to the subsequent Revelation, and in its secondary reality, to the first to believe in Him.

419 Today, according to the explicit words of the Point of the Bayān, souls who turn away from this wondrous Cause are deprived of the robe of names and attributes, and all are gathered and accounted as beasts before God. Say: Die in your rage, you who deny this grace and die upon hearing of it! He has indeed appeared in truth, and around Him circle the manifestations of the names and their kingdom, and then their realities and the things they designate, if you are among those who understand. He will never need anyone else, nor the names current among you. Rather, all stand in need of Him, and all ask for His bounty. Then the very beings of the names lament your injustice and your deeds, seek refuge in God from you and your kind, and address you, saying: “Heedless one! We were created and raised up by a command from God, the All-Powerful, the Almighty, the Omnipotent. We serve His presence, circle around His door, and glory in our relationship to Him, while He has ever been independent of us, if you are among those who perceive. Because you have become ablaze with the fire of envy and hatred, you have refused to allow Him, through whose command the kingdom of names was created, to ascribe to Himself any of those names. This is an injustice the like of which has never appeared in creation.” Then the peoples of the cities of eternity lament your injustice, you company of the malicious!

420 Say: He is the One whose servant the names glory in, should they be attributed to that servant or should he be called by them. Yet you have not perceived this within yourself and have been among those kept far away. Say: By God! I am indeed ʿAlī in the kingdom of eternity, Muḥammad in the dominion of names, then the Spirit in the cities of eternity, and then called Ḥusayn in this greatest Revelation. We have other names in the realms of ancient eternity of which no one is aware except God, the Unique, the All-Knowing, the All-Informed. Die in your rage, heedless one! The honor ordained for the names is due to their relationship to My Self, the Almighty, the All-Knowing. No name has been exalted in the world except by turning toward My sanctified, transcendent, mighty, unassailable presence. By My Self! Every name of goodness refers to My Self, and every wondrous mention leads to My beauty, if you are among those who are certain.

421 Even if you call someone other than Me by all the names, they will not apply to him. Rather, the realities of those names curse you as they leave your mouth, flee from you, and return to the most distant abode, this sanctified, inaccessible, exalted station. If you call darkness light, does that name apply to it? No, by the Lord of the worlds! Or if you call earthenware a pearl, does this name befit it? No, by your Lord, the All-Merciful, the Compassionate! Do you object to Him before whose countenance all names bow down, moving only by His permission and shining only through His radiant, transcendent, sanctified, resplendent, luminous glory? Fear God, do not follow your desires, and do not assail Him whom God has sanctified above every name and designation, and above every mention and exposition, and whom He has made, through His own Self, the guide to Himself, and through His own Essence, the acknowledger of His Essence, the Almighty, the Unassailable.

422 Your Lord has created the kingdom of names just as He creates everything, through His word “Be,” and it is, if you are among those who see. Should He wish, He would return them to utter nothingness. He indeed has power over whatever He wills, and He is the All-Knowing, the All-Wise. Listen to My words and turn in repentance to God, who created you to recognize His Self. Do not dispute His Cause after His appearance, and do not be among the malicious. Purify your heart, and then your ears, so that you may hear the melodies of God from His most pure, most luminous, truthful, trustworthy tongue. Do you imagine that anyone else can produce the like of what He has brought? No, by His Self, the All-Protecting, the Self-Subsisting! Or can anyone else speak before His face? No, by His beauty, the Almighty, the Beloved! He can never be confused with anyone else, and whatever proceeds from Him is sanctified above all that has appeared in the worlds, if only you knew.

423 Say: At one time He calls Himself by all the names, and at another He exalts Himself above them, above everything created between the heavens and the earth, and above all that has been and will be. Say: All affairs are in His grasp. By His command He does whatever He wills. Whoever says “Why?” or “By what means?” has disbelieved in God, the All-Powerful, the All-Protecting, the Self-Subsisting. Fear God and do not follow the path of the first to turn away from God. Shun him, then seek your Lord’s forgiveness, so that there may blow upon your inner reality that which will dispel the odors of these people. He is indeed the Ever-Forgiving, the Most Kind. If you listen, it is for your own good; if you deny and turn away, it is to your own detriment. Your Lord, the All-Merciful, is independent of all that exists, and He is the Sanctified, the Most Exalted, the Almighty, the All-Praised.

424 Know that every occurrence of the word Bahāʾ in the words of God was revealed for a particular, distinct, visible, manifest, unmistakable person. Today all mercy is for whoever believes in Him, and all retribution is for those souls who do not become certain of Him and turn away. Indeed, those names that you have heard concerning the first to turn away and have referred to him were all reserved for this Revelation. Those who turn away have distorted them without realizing it. This injustice comes from none other than that root of the tree of rejection and corruption.

425 Beyond all this, even if, as you and your guides suppose, this Revelation were not mentioned in the Bayān and the word Bahāʾ had not been revealed in the Book, what harm would that do to the Revelation? He says: “I have appeared with the same proof as the Primal Point—indeed, a greater one, if only you perceived.” By God, besides whom there is no god, He has never wished anyone to argue in support of His Cause through anything other than the Revelation itself and the verses revealed. Your question would be legitimate only if the Bearer of Revelation were to say, “My evidence is My name.” Exalted is He above that! The evidence has always been and will always be the Revelation itself, and besides it, whatever has proceeded from Him and descended from His presence. This is the proof with which all the Prophets and Messengers have been sent. Whoever turns away has turned away from God, his Creator, disbelieved in His verses, and been recorded by the Pen of God upon the Tablet as one of those who associate partners with Him.

426 The Point of the Bayān Himself—may the spirit of all besides Him be sacrificed for Him—likewise offered no proof other than verses. Thus, when they brought Him before numerous assemblies and demanded proof, nothing but verses appeared from that sanctuary of names and attributes. Whenever any of His servants requested evidence and proof, the answer was given: “Look to the divine verses,” for the proof has always been the verses of God, and this proof alone suffices for everyone on earth. In this Most Great Revelation, most people have witnessed whatever they desired, whether divinely revealed verses the like of which no one has seen, learned expositions, or extraordinary feats. Nevertheless, they have disbelieved in the reality, essence, being, and very Self of the Primal Point, and do not realize it. Now you bring up the names! People have always turned away from the known Sovereign and toward the imaginings of their own selves.

427 As for what he has written—“If it concerns a specifically named individual and a particular person, where in the Bayān is this, and in what words?”—solely for the sake of God, this servant will therefore mention some of the revealed verses that have descended from the heaven of the will of the Most Exalted One concerning this blessed, most mighty, most ancient Name and this most unassailable, most holy Revelation. Yet, by God, besides whom there is no god, the All-Glorious Beauty has never designated anything as the proof and standard of His Cause except the revealed verses that descend from the heaven of divine unity. Although you have raised these very words as an objection to God and have never intended to understand, this servant will nevertheless mention some of the words of the Revealer of the Bayān, to make God’s proof complete. Even if you are not guided by them, just as you were not guided before, perhaps other servants will be guided by them. Although He Himself is the proof, I wish only that you yourself should realize that you are among those of whom He says: “Even if they see every sign, they will not believe in it.” And elsewhere, His words—exalted is He: “Those against whom your Lord’s word has come into effect will not believe, even if every sign comes to them, until they see the painful punishment.”

428 For the Sun has risen and shines with a hundred thousand radiances and lights, and the Point of the Bayān likewise says in all His Tablets: “Recognize Him through Himself and through His verses.” Indeed, it is forbidden for any soul to adduce proof of Him through anything other than His most holy, most unassailable Self and what appears from Him. Yet this servant takes upon himself this transgression and has looked, and still looks, to the Sovereign of Eternity for pardon. If this servant wished to mention everything concerning the matter he has asked about, tablets would not suffice, for countless utterances have descended from the dominion of everlasting life.

429 A few passages will therefore suffice. They are as follows, in His words—exalted be His majesty: “Indeed, in like manner We sent down the Qurʾān before, but you were veiled from My intent. That is what night and day revolved around for eight vāḥids, and through it you profess unity in worship; yet you remained veiled from its mystery after it had been fulfilled. That is the standard of guidance in the Bayān, in which you believe until the time when the Sun of Bahāʾ rises. That is the Revelation of God. If you act according to it, you are indeed believers and will abide forever in Riḍwān; otherwise, you will perish.” End of quotation.

430 Today the Revelation of God is manifest, and the Sun of Bahāʾ has risen from the highest horizon and shines, while those who turn away are perishing and utterly nonexistent, as you can see, if you are fair-minded. Thus He was living in the All-Glorious Horizon, if you can be among those who hear or those who see. I adjure you by God, who has caused all things to speak in praise of Himself: after this decisive utterance, this firmly established verse, and this revelation of divine glory, is any room left for a soul to turn away or hesitate? No, by His own true Self, if only you knew! Elsewhere He says—His words, exalted be His glory and mighty His exaltation: “From the first rising of the Sun of Bahāʾ until its setting is better, in the Book of God, than all the night, if you perceive. God has created nothing except [corrupted original text], for all labor to attain the presence of God and then His good pleasure.” End of quotation.

431 The purest sight is needed to behold the Most Great Vision and apprehend the meaning of the divine words. Listen to the call of the Sovereign of the Bayān, who has spoken of the next Revelation with complete explicitness, so that perhaps those embodiments of misguidance may not hesitate after its appearance or see any likeness, peer, or equal to the Manifestation of divine unity. For the oneness of the Essence is established only through the Manifestation of Revelation, and God’s transcendence above likenesses and equals is demonstrated only through the Manifestation of Revelation. If any likeness, rival, or equal is envisaged for the Manifestation of Revelation, the transcendence of the eternal Essence above all likeness is not established, nor is His own sanctity above duality demonstrated. Feel shame before God, and assign Him no partner in sovereignty. He is one in His Essence, and God is witness to what I say. In this connection He says—His words, exalted be His majesty: “Noon has indeed drawn near, yet you do not recognize that Day. As for Him whose presence is My very presence, do not consent for Him to what no soul would consent to for itself.” End of quotation.

432 Despite this, all have overstepped their bounds and paid no heed to the divine counsels. They have consented to what the Jews did not consent to for the Spirit, nor the people of the Qurʾān for the Point of the Bayān. This comes only from ignorance and immaturity. If you have not heard until now, hear now, and feel shame before God. Turn from the left hand of error to the right hand of guidance. Before long, you and those like you, and everything you have uttered, will perish, just as you are perished today, for all things testify to the extinction of any soul who is not remembered in the presence of God. Purify yourself from the world, do not say what you have not learned, and do not mention what you have not understood. Know your limits and your measure, and do not exceed your station.

433 He states explicitly that the appearance of the Sun at its noonday zenith is near, and that you do not recognize or apprehend that Day, just as you have neither apprehended nor recognized it. In speaking of the next Revelation, that eternal Beauty has never spoken otherwise than explicitly, unlike the earlier Books, which all spoke in abstruse allusions. In the Revelation of the year sixty, the fingers of power tore through all those veils and spoke of the next Revelation throughout the Bayān explicitly, without allusion.

434 The matter He has left to the sight and discernment of His servants is this: He has spoken variously of its nearness and remoteness. Although inwardly all these differing words refer to a single point and no difference whatsoever is envisaged, the measure of outward difference in His statements has been for the sake of distinction. Thus, in one passage He has mentioned Mustaghāth, while in another He says that it will not reach Mustaghāth, and expresses the hope that the Word of God will be raised up before then. Elsewhere He says that no one knows of the Revelation except God, and that whenever it occurs, all must acknowledge the Point of Truth. In another passage He says that if He wishes to appear at this very moment, He is free to do so, and no one has the right to say why or wherefore. To one of the Letters who asked about that Most Great Revelation, He says: “Perhaps you will attain His presence.” Likewise, a Tablet descended from the heaven of His will, and He directed that it be perused in the school of the next Revelation.

435 All these apparent differences of wording are resolved by a decisive utterance. He gives a most emphatic warning that, when that desired Essence appears, you should not look to what has been revealed in the Bayān, but rather to the Revelation itself and what appears from Him. Those endowed with sight know that before and after, nearness and remoteness, have never existed and will never exist in the court of God. Thus, if He wishes to bring forth the time of the first and the last in a single instant, He has always been and will always be able to do so, for these limitations pertain to creation, just as the fifty thousand years of the Resurrection elapsed in an hour.

436 You who are senseless with the wine of ignorance and heedlessness! How long will you walk in darkness and act according to your own desires? Detach your hearts from human limitations and look toward the seat of the Sovereign of Oneness, so that perhaps you may enter the station of “Know God through God” and be free of how and why. Do not ask the Sun, “What is your name, and what is the evidence of your light?”

437 You who are without sight! The evidence of its radiance has always been and will always be its radiance, and the proof of its light, its light. Listen to the word of the All-Knowing, the All-Informed, who has explicitly foretold that in this holy and everlasting Revelation all perish except those who hold fast to the Ark of Eternity in this Most Great Revelation. They are indeed the people of the Ark of Holiness in the sight of God, the All-Powerful, the Most High, the Most Great. Whoever holds fast to this Ark is saved, and whoever turns away is drowned. This is indeed a revelation from One who is All-Knowing, All-Informed.

438 His words—exalted be His glory and great His independence: “O concourse of lights! By God, the True One, We do not speak from desire, nor do We send down a single letter of that Book except by the permission of God, the True One. Fear God and do not doubt the Cause of God, for the mystery of this Gate is concealed beneath the cloud of the written line and inscribed above the veil of concealment by the hands of God, the Lord of concealment and of the written line. Around that Gate God has indeed created seas of the water of the elixir, reddened with the oil of existence, and life through the intended fruit. God has ordained for it ships of moist red ruby, and none but the people of Bahāʾ embark upon them, by the permission of God, the Most High. He is God, ever mighty and wise. There the angels of the cloud, in the eight souls, bear the throne of God, and the decree is witnessed in the Mother Book. The guilty will then be brought before the All-Merciful around the throne, in truth and by the Truth, symbolically.” End of quotation.

439 Had you possessed understanding, these very revealed verses sent down in the Qayyūm of Books would have led you all to sacrifice your lives in the path of this universal divine Revelation. Yet understanding has been taken away from all souls such as these because of what your hands have earned. Thus, in this spiritual springtime, this sovereign of seasons and these days of God, you have been deprived of the outpourings of the All-Merciful and the April rain of the wisdom of the All-Glorious. You have remained far from the good pleasure of the all-knowing King and are occupied, like animals, with grazing in the wilderness of desires and the garden of allusions.

440 In short, today no one is accounted worthy of mention before God except those who ride in this Ark of eternal glory. These souls are rarer than red sulfur. Soon you will see most people, without realizing it, submerged in the depths of the ocean of ignorance and rejection, and deprived of the divine Ark, except for a numbered few. It remains to be seen when the winds of grace will blow, pass over the bodies of those dead in self and desire, give them life, and turn them toward the realm of oneness.

441 Then, where he says that the angels of the cloud, in eight souls, bear the throne of the Lord, this is itself clear proof that only a few of the people of the Bayān will become certain of this Revelation, as he indicated by mentioning eight. For such has been the case at the beginning of every Revelation; it has not been, nor is it, peculiar to this Revelation. The throne in this passage has meant, and will mean, the Cause of God, the Word of God, and the first utterance of the Tongue of God. It is that Word which strikes terror into all who are in the heavens and on earth, and causes all who are in the dominion of command and creation to fall thunderstruck. It is that Word which has been named the Most Great Weight in the Tablets of God, and not every soul is capable of bearing it. It is that Word at whose appearance all kindreds lament and the foundations of all things are shaken. No one is capable of bearing this throne except the angels of the cloud, who, with God’s assistance, bear this most great Trust. Through accepting it, the embodiments of the Cause of God appear, and the most wondrous, most great Word speaks.

442 In one station, the throne is the Manifestation himself, who is visible among the people, for that sign of divine unity and essence of singleness has ever been alone in his own seat, with no one beside him: “God was, and there was nothing with him.” In that station, the throne is the Manifestation himself, and the eight angels point to this blessed, most wondrous and most inaccessible Name, for its numerical value corresponds to that of Bahāʾ without the hamza. In the primal reality, none bears that throne except his own sanctified Self.

443 At this time none but his own Self can bear his throne—hear this from me.

444 This couplet is among the verses that flowed from the most wondrous, most sweet Tongue in Iraq.

445 In another station, the throne is the first to believe, the first to enter the divine presence, the first to be raised up, and the first to bear the burden. He is the first of creation, the first to speak, the first to see, the first to hear, the first to recognize, the first to be certain, and the first to be informed. His hearing is the gate of divine hearing for all existence, and any hearing that has not entered that gate is not numbered among those that hear, but is accounted deaf before God. Likewise, his sight is the gate of divine sight for all souls; his knowledge is the gate of divine knowledge among God’s creatures; his power is the gate of eternal power; his tongue is the gate of the divine tongue; his heart is the gate of the divine heart; and his recognition is the gate of divine recognition. Through his speech, what was concealed is revealed; at his request, the Kawthar of life is poured forth; through his turning toward God, the cloud of the All-Merciful’s mercy bestows its grace; and through his listening, the divine Word emerges from the treasury of the knowledge of oneness. If you attain a measure of understanding, through this very grace and favor bestowed upon the First Remembrance, the Primal Word, the First Pearl, the Pillar of Decree, and the Hidden Mystery of the Cloud, you will gain some awareness of the favor, mercy, generosity, and grace of the True One.

446 In short, the blind bear no resemblance to those who see, nor do the deaf have any affinity with those who hear. Endless meanings lie concealed in this revealed verse, but until someone is found who thirsts for the Salsabīl of meanings, the Cupbearer of oneness will not offer the everlasting cups of spiritual meaning. Consider how explicitly the condition of those who turn away and those who are guilty has been revealed in the Book of God, and how the meaning of the blessed revealed verse has become manifest. Those guilty ones have been, and will be, among the people of the Bayān, and whatever harm befalls the Beauty of the All-Merciful will come from the people of the Bayān, just as what befell the Point of the Bayān came only from the people of the Qurʾān. Yet, by God, besides whom there is no other god, at every moment this Essence of Eternity has suffered what no one else has suffered. Had the people of the Bayān possessed sight, that blessed verse alone, “I am indeed alive in the All-Glorious Horizon,” would have sufficed them all.

447 Likewise, may my soul and all who are in the heavens and on earth be sacrificed for him: he left nothing undone in counseling the people of the Bayān and conveying his message to them. In no age or era has the preceding Manifestation spoken of the subsequent Manifestation with such detail and explicitness, as you all witness and attest, if you judge fairly. Listen to the words of this servant and rescue yourself from darkness upon darkness. Do not, for the sake of two fleeting days, make yourself deserving of endless torment, and do not occupy yourself with distorting the religion of God and his Cause.

448 This couplet is fitting here: “Do not, for the body’s welfare, weave irreligion around religion; do not deceitfully sow the seed of the world in the field of faith.”

449 I bring all the revealed verses to a close with these most wondrous, most inaccessible, most holy and most exalted words, which have descended from the heaven of the glory of the Primal Point, so that their holy fragrances may perfume those who seek the sanctuary of the All-Merciful and those who love the Beauty of the All-Glorious. Although souls such as these may not perceive their fragrances, they are mentioned with regard for the souls who yearn. I swear by the King of all horizons that every ear that hears them will attain a wondrous life and cease listening to the words of anyone but God, and every heart that understands them will assuredly become a wellspring of God’s boundless knowledge. Nothing more explicit is possible to mention, and nothing clearer has yet appeared from the kingdom of utterance.

450 How closely this mention and praise by the Primal Point resembles the account related of the departed Shaykh Aḥmad—upon him be the glory of God. On a journey to perform the pilgrimage, he said to a certain Ḥājī Muḥammad Ismāʿīl, who lived in the land of Ṣād, “Convey my greetings to the Bāb,” mentioning him by his blessed name. The Shaykh’s purpose was nothing but to guide that soul and other souls. After hearing the Shaykh’s words, that man was bewildered as to who was intended and why these good words had issued from his blessed tongue. He returned from the pilgrimage, and some years passed. When the Most Exalted One arrived in the land of Ṣād and talk of him spread among the people—that someone had appeared and claimed to be the Bāb—the man then realized what the Shaykh had meant.

451 Yet people such as you have not been, and will not be, awakened by these clear, manifest, radiant and luminous statements. His words—exalted be his glory: “Do not put yourselves ahead of the people among whom the Point will appear, if they are believers. Say: They are the best of those on earth, and had God known any better than them in faith, he would have caused him to appear from among them. Convey greetings from God to his father and his mother, to whatever was with him, and to those of his kindred who believe in him. If you show kindness to every soul, perhaps you will grasp this before he appears; afterward you will indeed grasp it and know. Upon you, O Bahāʾuʾllāh, and then upon your kindred, be the remembrance of God and the praise of all things at every moment, before every moment and after every moment.” End of quotation.

452 The Point of the Bayān’s purpose in these verses was to identify the origin of the Manifestation. Now, you who are without fairness, consider what courtesy he enjoined. He tells the people of the Bayān not to put themselves ahead of the people from whom that Primal Point will appear. He says, “If they are believers.” By this final statement, the bond of kinship of those related to this Manifestation who today turn away from the True One is severed, as he has explicitly stated. He says that those people are the best of the peoples on earth, for had there been a better people on earth, that Countenance of oneness and divine Being would certainly have appeared from among them. He commands the people of the Bayān to convey greetings from God to the father and mother of that Most Great Luminary, and likewise to those of his kindred who believe in him.

453 It would be no surprise, given the foolishness of the people of the Bayān, if they were to say, “To whom do the divine greetings return, when the father and mother are no longer living?” O sightless ones! The very moment the greeting descended from the tongue of the Lord of the worlds, it reached its destination. This emphasis in the Bayān was intended to ensure that nothing should occur during the Manifestation to sadden that Essence of Eternity. After these divine counsels, the prescribed courtesies, and these exalted, unassailable words, that most holy and pure Being offered remembrance and praise, turning toward the seat of the Manifestation and his kindred. By God, this heavenly stream of utterance has flowed from the tongue of the All-Merciful with such sweetness that, if all who are in the heavens and on earth were to partake of a drop of it purely for the sake of God, they would all become intoxicated with that divine wine, turn toward the Eternal One, and see everything other than him as nonexistent, lost, and utter nothingness.

454 By the explicit text of the Most Exalted Countenance—may the soul of all else be sacrificed for him—all were commanded to offer praise and greetings at the time of the Manifestation. Yet no one has succeeded in doing so; they have regarded the divine counsel as futile and erased his testament from their hearts. Indeed, although they have not entered the presence of the all-knowing Sovereign with praise and greetings, they have assailed him from every side with spears and arrows. Authority belongs to God, before and after, and upon him his own Self and Essence bestow praise, if you are among those who understand. Since no one succeeded in conveying this praise on behalf of the All-Merciful, the divine Being has himself, in this wondrous, merciful Manifestation, conveyed remembrance and praise to his own Self on behalf of God. His words—exalted be his praise: “O Beloved of Bahāʾ! I have conveyed your remembrance and praise, and then the praise of all things, from you to myself, so that your word may be established among the worlds and your signs made manifest to those who understand. And now I say: Upon you, O Beloved of Bahāʾ, be the remembrance of God and his praise, then the praise of the dwellers in the highest Assembly, and the praise of the dwellers in the cities of eternity, then the praise of the dwellers in the oceans of grandeur, then the praise of all the worlds, at every moment, before every moment, after every moment, and at the very moment of each moment.”

455 At this point, these wondrous and most sweet words descended from the all-glorious Dominion in prayer to God, the Exalted, the Most High: “Upon you, O Glory of God, Beloved of Bahāʾ, be the remembrance of God and his glory, then the glory of the dwellers in the highest Assembly, then the glory of the dwellers in the cities of eternity, then the glory of all things, then the glory of your own Self, for yourself, through yourself, and the glory of this Bahāʾ who has appeared to aid you among the worlds. O Beloved of Bahāʾ! By your might and majesty, you have fallen short in neither conveying your message to your servants nor ordering the affairs of your creation. In all this you have desired only their submission before the sovereignty of your oneness and their humility at the appearances of the lights of your countenance. By your might, O my Beloved, I find myself abashed before all that you have conveyed for my manifestation, for you have breathed only in remembrance of me, spoken only to establish my Cause, and nothing has flowed from your pen in which your purpose was not my remembrance and praise. In every circumstance, there has appeared from you that which points to me, speaks explicitly of my manifestation, and tells of my beauty.”

456 “Yet how can I remember you, O my Beloved, when, by your might, the succession of adversities and the unceasing calamities leave me no opportunity to weep for myself, much less to praise your exalted and mighty Self? Whenever I wish to praise you or draw near to you through the wonders of your remembrance, the malice of your enemies and the aversion of the tyrants among your creation prevent me. If I turn to the right, I find the book of Sijjīn from one of your creatures, containing what has shaken the foundations of all things and the foundations of the kingdom of your most beautiful names. Whenever I turn to the left, I find a book from the wicked, containing what has caused the inmost realities of your chosen ones and the hearts of your prophets to cry out, lament, and mourn. This, even though you commanded the people of the Bayān—who profess faith in you, acknowledge your singleness, and claim submission at the appearances of the lights of your glorious unity—that every soul should write, in every Vāḥid, a book establishing your Cause in this Manifestation, through which the sun of might and power has shone from the horizon of the heaven of your glorious mercy, so that your servants might be reminded by it and await this announcement of which you gave them glad tidings in all your Tablets, your scriptures, and the scrolls of your glory, through your true words addressed to the people of the Bayān: ‘In every Vāḥid, you are to write to one another a book establishing the truth of the One whom we shall manifest, so that perhaps, on the day of his appearance, you may act according to what you write.’”

457 “O my God, they have cast your ordinances behind them and forgotten the counsels you gave them in your Tablets. If only they had been content with that! Instead, each of them has written a book that has set ablaze your own Self, the realities of all things, and the inmost hearts of those among your loved ones who are near to you and those among your chosen ones who are sanctified. They have spread it throughout the lands to destroy the sanctity of God among his creatures, the majesty of God among his creation, and reverence for him among his servants. By your might, O my Beloved, no morning has come upon me without my gaze falling upon something that has burned my inmost heart, agitated my soul, and saddened my heart. Thus I have wept with the eyes of my innermost being, and with my weeping have wept the eyes of those whom you have honored among your creatures, chosen from among your creation, and made the recipients of your revelation, the treasuries of your knowledge, the manifestations of your Cause, the dawning places of your power, the repositories of your inspiration, and the daysprings of the suns of your bounty.”

458 “No evening has come upon me without there befalling me something that has withheld the breezes of your mercy from all contingent things and closed the doors of your grace upon all created beings. By your might, O my Beloved, I am bewildered by my plight. You see me now like a fish thrashing upon the dust, and you hear the cry of my heart, O you in whose hand is the dominion of signs. All this has befallen me after I called them toward your gifts and favors, acquainted them with the paths of your Cause and your good pleasure, and commanded them to bow humbly at the door of your mercy and enter the court of your singleness. Whenever I called to them, O my God, with the wondrous words and the gems of your verses that you inspired in me, they rose up in opposition to a degree that no one can reckon. You alone have reckoned it through your sovereignty and knowledge.”

459 “Now, O Beloved of Bahāʾ, giver of life to Bahāʾ, awakener of Bahāʾ’s longing, remembrance of Bahāʾ, and dear one of Bahāʾ, weep for the person of Bahāʾ! By God, he has remained alone among your creatures and solitary among your servants. They do to him whatever they wish, and I have no helper to restrain them from their deeds or drive them away from your sacred sanctuary and the pavilion of your might and majesty. Praise be to you in all this and in all that has befallen me in your path. Although your servants have stripped from my body the garment of joy and the robe of gladness, majesty, and honor, you have nevertheless bestowed on me through your grace that which no one can lay hands upon, even should all the creatures of the heavens and the earth unite against me. Praise be to you for what you have bestowed on me through your generosity, O Beloved of my heart and the desire of the hearts of those who know you. Through my love for you, I will not be dismayed by anything, even should the clouds of destiny rain down upon me the arrows of affliction. I have committed my affairs to you and placed my trust in you. You are sufficient for me, my helper and my supporter, and through you I have become independent of all creatures. Praise be to you, for you are the one I worship and the one worshipped by all who are in the worlds.” End of quotation.

460 If only they would allow the Beauty of the Lord of the worlds time to occupy himself with the praise and remembrance of God! By God, because of the oppression of those who associate partners with him, he has found no opportunity even to lament his own plight, much less to offer remembrance and praise. Yet the Point of the Bayān commanded all the people of the Bayān to write to one another in every Vāḥid, and in that book to remind one another of this Most Great Manifestation through wondrous expressions of remembrance. That book was to be a reminder establishing the Cause of God and awakening those souls, so that all might be prepared to acknowledge and submit at the appearance of the Primal Will and the all-embracing Word, and the establishment of the Eternal Being upon the throne of mercy. Thus he said—exalted be his remembrance: “In every Vāḥid, you are to write to one another a book establishing the truth of the One whom we shall manifest, for on the day of his appearance you will surely act according to what you write.” But what the people of the Bayān have actually done is what is now witnessed. At the appointed time of the divine promise, they have all written tablets refuting the Beauty of Oneness and sent them to one another; some have also sent them to the court of the throne. Among these is the book of Sijjīn by that heedless soul, which has been brought into this presence. Shame on your fidelity! Shame on your modesty! Shame on your courtesy! Shame on what your hands have wrought, O company of the heedless!

461 Consider, O people of the Bayān, how much that Sovereign of the throne of destiny has done to prepare the way. It is just as people teach birds to speak; indeed, by God, he has taught the people of the Bayān far more than this, devoting his blessed time to ensuring that, at the time of the Manifestation, no one should do anything that would grieve that tender, gentle, radiant heart. Thus, in one passage, after giving compassionate, firm, and well-founded counsels, he says—exalted be his glory: “His likeness—exalted be his remembrance—is that of the sun. If infinitely many mirrors face him, they will all reflect the sun’s radiance according to their capacity. And if none faces it, the sun will still rise and set, while the veiling belongs to the mirrors. I have spared no effort in counseling these people and preparing them to turn toward God, their Lord, and to believe in God, their Maker.” End of quotation.

462 Now consider what provision he made in that statement in which he instructed them to write, at the beginning of every Vāḥid, a book establishing the truth of the next Manifestation, and likewise in this statement in which he says that, if infinitely many mirrors face the sun, its radiance will assuredly appear according to the capacity of those mirrors; and if all remain veiled, the sun will still rise and shine in the fullness of its light, splendor, and radiance. As is evident, its outpouring upon all the mirrors, and the reflection of all of them from it, add nothing to its worth; likewise, if all remain veiled from it, no deficiency befalls it. The purpose of this statement by that Beauty of the All-Merciful is that the people of the Bayān should not cling to the mirrors on the day when the Sun of inner meanings appears and shines forth, for the existence and nonexistence of all the mirrors are alike before that inaccessible, exalted Sun.

463 If only the Primal Point were here now to witness that you have taken mirrors for yourselves and made them the standard for recognizing the One without whom their realities and their very beings would not have been created! You have gone to such excess in the Cause of God that you have made your acknowledgment of him dependent on the acknowledgment of veiled mirrors. Woe to you, O company of the unjust and the heedless! Despite these clear and manifest statements and these firm and well-founded provisions made by that Sun of the glory of oneness, after the completion of the first Vāḥid and the appearance of the Luminary of the horizons, all broke the Covenant of God, rose up in hypocrisy, and forgot the divine provisions and counsels. It is as though they had made a mockery of them. Woe to them for what they have committed before the face of God, the Most High, the Most Great!

464 As for your having written that, if the revealed descriptions concern the name Bahāʾ, this name is one of the names of God, and that the praises of the names of God are not exclusive to this blessed name, you also mentioned Mīrzā Kamāl and Mīrzā Jamāl in mockery of God. Through this you have become one of the first to mock the nature of the Wondrous Creator, then the Friend, then the One who conversed with God. By God, you are the first to have mocked the Spirit, then to have mocked Muḥammad, the Messenger of God, then to have mocked ʿAlī when he caused the dawn of inner meanings to break and the sun of utterance to shine through the sovereignty of God, the All-Protecting, the Self-Subsisting. You were the first to mock this Manifestation, through whom everything sent down from all eternity was established, through whom every soul ascended to the heights of realities, and every assured believer entered the Paradise of God, the All-Powerful, the Almighty, the Beloved. Take up your pen, you who mock God! By God, because of it the Most Exalted Pen has lamented, and with it all that has been and all that will be. Everything you have committed goes back and ultimately leads to the pen of the first to turn away from God, the One, the Unique, the King, the All-Protecting, the Self-Subsisting.

465 Indeed, the reality of his pen has fled from him and appeared before God, crying out with such a cry that everything bearing the name of a thing wept over it. It lamented before its Lord and then complained of its owner, if you are among those who understand. One day, the reality of the pen of the one who has turned away from God appeared before the Throne and lamented with such a lament that the inhabitants of the celestial assembly wept over its plight. It is astonishing that you did not hear or heed the lamentation and plaintive cries of all things at that moment. From that day until now, all the sanctified ones and those brought near, and indeed the realities of all things, have been occupied with lamentation, mourning, and anguish. Here it has become necessary for me to recount the prayer it offered before the Throne, so that receptive souls may, from the lament of that wronged one, perceive the wrongs suffered by the Beloved of all creation—what has befallen that Most Holy Beauty through the oppression of the oppressors among the people of the Bayān. This is how the Pen addressed its Lord, the Lord of all things and the Lord of the worlds:

466 “Glory be to you, O God, my God and my Beloved! You are the One at whose command the sound of the Most Exalted Pen rose between earth and heaven. Through it you decreed upon the Tablets what you decreed through your guarded knowledge and your irrevocable command; and through what you inscribed with it upon the Tablet, you bring forth in creation what was ordained by your will and decreed by your purpose. Now, my God, hear the cry of this pen, which has become far removed from the shore of nearness to you and meeting with you, and has been afflicted between the fingers of one of the most wretched of your creatures. By your might, my Beloved, I am afraid of the force of your wrath and the manifestations of your vengeance. You now witness the agitation of my soul, the trembling of my heart, and the turmoil of my body, because you have placed me in subjection to the fingers of this one who associates partners with you, who has not inhaled the fragrances of fairness from the paradise of your justice and your gifts, nor sensed the sweet breezes from the garment of your grace and your loving-kindness.

467 “Now, my God, by your might, if you command me, I will assuredly tear open his belly through the power ordained within my inmost being, so that my soul may perhaps find rest and my heart be still. For he has enlisted my aid in rending the veil of your majesty and sanctity, and has committed that which has burned the hearts of your chosen ones within the pavilion of your glory, and the hearts of your trusted ones in the chambers of your sanctified might and the tents of your holy detachment. I ask you, my Beloved, by your sovereignty, and by your name, your manifestation, and your grandeur: if you do not command me to do what I have asked of you, deliver me from this wicked one and his fingers.

468 “Alas, alas, my Beloved! You created me to remember you and praise your very self, yet I have been deprived of that for which I was created. From me has proceeded that which has torn the veil of majesty and grandeur, changed the faces of the inhabitants of the celestial assembly, reduced to nothing the realities of those in the kingdom of names, and shaken the foundations of the cities of eternity. How, then, my God, can I raise my head among the pens, because of the shame that makes me bow my head in the presence of those brought near, at the manifestations of the lights of your beauty, and in the gathering of those who affirm your oneness in the court of the gate of your majesty? By your might, even if you clothe me in the robe of forgiveness, the breezes of your name, the All-Merciful, blow upon me from the direction of your loving care, you immerse me in the oceans of your tenderness and loving-kindness, and you forgive me for what I have committed in your days—by your might, my heart will not be still and my soul will not find peace. Suppose, my Beloved, that through your grace and loving care you have cleansed me of disobedience: how can the odors of shame leave me, when they blow from my own self upon my own self at every moment and have deprived me of the breezes of the Paradise you have placed above all gardens?

469 “Even if you, my God and my Master, pardon me through your forbearance and forgive my sin through your grace and generosity, how can I raise my head before the city of your might and your presence? By your might, I have reached such a degree of abasement that, if you looked upon me with the glances of your compassion, you yourself would weep over this wretched one, who has lost hope in himself and in every direction because he has been deprived of the flashing lights of your beauty that shone from the horizon of the heaven of your might and your will. Alas, alas for my despair on this day when you have opened the gates of union with you to those in your earth and your heaven, and called everyone to the seat of nearness to you and meeting with you! Alas, alas for what has been ordained for me in the tablets of your decree, through which you have effaced my portion and the sweetness of my communion with you while beholding the lights of your face! If only I had been deprived of all that you ordained for the good of your creatures and had not associated with those who neither knew you nor bowed down before you, and from whom, with my assistance, proceeded that which burned the hearts of your trusted and chosen ones!

470 “If I say that this has befallen me because I deserved it—by your might, my Beloved, I have found no stability for myself when the winds of your decree blow, no existence for my being before the city of the sovereignty of your power and might, and no mention of myself at the manifestations of the glory of your oneness. How can one who has no existence before your manifestation and your power move without your permission and your will?

471 “And if I say that you have afflicted me with this without my deserving it, this would be an act of rebellion on my part against you, for I have not learned the mysteries of your Cause or the workings of your wisdom and decree. Thus I have become bewildered about my plight. My understanding will never ascend to the heavens of your wisdom, which you have concealed from the sight of your creatures and your creation, nor will the bird of my knowledge fly into the atmosphere of your knowledge, which you have hidden in the treasuries of your infallibility. Thus my agitation increases, and my sorrow, distress, and affliction grow more severe.

472 Ah, ah, my Beloved! How can I recount what I witness of the manifestations of your action and the workings of your Cause? At one time I see that you have taken a pen like me in the fingers of your mercy and made it a companion to your own self and an intimate of your beauty. You take it in the fingers of your might and majesty and cause oceans of life to flow from it, with a drop of which you have quickened the realities of contingent existence and the hearts of the inhabitants of all worlds. Through its scratching you have drawn to yourself the hearts of those brought near and the souls of the sincere. At another time I see that you have afflicted me with the fingers of those who associate partners with you and made me powerless beneath their fingers. From me has appeared that which has troubled the hearts of the Concourse on high and the inhabitants of the cities of eternity, and has entangled the inmost hearts of those whose faces you have honored by turning them away from all but you, whom you have sanctified from the allusions of the people of your earth, and whom you have drawn near beneath the shadow of your care and bounty. By your might, I fear that my disobedience will bring the Pen of your Cause to a halt and leave the tablets of your decree and the pages of your ordainment idle. If only I had not existed and had never been mentioned!

473 By your might, my God, I will find no existence for myself after this. If you do not return me to nonexistence, then judge between me and this oppressor, whose action has altered my face among the sincere among your creatures and those of your loved ones who have been brought near. At that very hour, my God, I fled from him to you and came to you with the clamor of the bereaved, the cry of the disobedient, and the lamentation of sinners. Judge, then, between me and him by the sovereignty of your decree and the dominion of your justice and power. Is there any judge besides you, my Beloved, with whom I may seek refuge and to whom I may flee? No, by your might! There is no refuge but you and nowhere to flee except to you.

474 This is a wronged one who has stood before the city of your justice and mercy; this is a poor one who has clung to the hem of your wealth; this is a deprived one who has sought shelter in the shadow of the sanctuary of your holiness and kindness. I ask you by your name through which you shattered the idols of illusion and desire, adorned those brought near with the robes of your recognition and forgiveness, and stripped from those who associate partners with you the mantle of your names and kindness, to send down from the clouds of your power and the heaven of your grace that which will calm my heart and bring peace to my soul.

475 Ah, ah, my Beloved! Agitation and distress have seized me while the throne of your name, the Unconstrained, stands firm. Even if you were to torment the oppressor who wronged me for as long as your sovereignty and kingdom endure, my soul would not rejoice at it, my desolation would not depart, and my agitation and distress would not be stilled. For my face, my God, has grown pale with shame at what has proceeded from me. This is a scent that will never leave me, even if the winds of your forgiveness blow upon me from the right hand of your care for as long as the glory of your oneness endures. Do you see, then, my Beloved, anyone more bereft than I, or anyone more abased?

476 Throughout all these days, my God, I longed for reunion with you, so that I slept at night in the hope of meeting you and raised my head in the morning only out of longing for your presence. I remained in that state until the winds of your decree stirred from the right hand of your will, the manifestations of your ordainment appeared from the horizon of your decree, and I was turned away from you toward your enemies. Ah, ah, for this blowing! Ah, ah, for this passing! Ah, ah, for these winds sent forth, which seized me through your power and left me in the presence of those who associate partners with your own self and oppose your beauty! If only they had been content with what they committed in your days and inflicted upon your own self! No, by your might! They will find no rest until they shed your blood among your creatures and devour your flesh with the fangs of hatred in the kingdom of creation. These are the wretched ones from whose pride and arrogance Pharaoh flees, from whose tyranny and oppression Nimrod runs away, and from whose evil and malice Satan seeks refuge in you.

477 Ah, ah, my Beloved! When I sprouted on the shore of the holiness of your oneness, I called upon you by your name, the Compassionate, then by your name, the All-Merciful, then by your name, the Merciful, then by your name, the All-Forgiving, then by your name, the Tenderhearted, then by your name, the All-Loving, then by your name, the All-Sufficing, then by your name, the Bestower. Whenever I found my soul sorrowful, I cheered it with the glad news of nearness to you and of meeting you, and whenever it grew agitated, I calmed it by recalling the days of reunion with you. When my growth was complete, one of your servants uprooted me from my place, and I was passed from hand to hand and from market to market until I reached the market known to you through your knowledge, which encompasses all things. Then one of your creatures and created beings bought me. But, my God and my Beloved, by your might, when he took me in his hand, I saw that he was unaware of this. I was bewildered within myself at this, for this clamor arose from me involuntarily.

478 In the innermost secrecy I called upon you with the call of those who yearn and the cry of those who hope, asking you to disclose to me the secret of this and what you had concealed from me behind the veils of your ordainment and the splendors of your decree, until he carried me to the land where you were established upon the throne of your wronged condition. I perceived the fragrance of the shirt from its dust and the breaths of holiness from its air. Joy and gladness so seized me that I saw myself upon the heights of might and majesty, as though I had become ruler over the highest kings of the realms of eternity and sovereign over all who dwell in the kingdom of creation.

479 I remained in that state until the one carrying me reached a road that branched into two paths, one to the right and one to the left. He turned from the right toward the left. Ah, ah! Then I felt agitation within me and was terrified, and everything was terrified at my terror, until he brought me to a door. When it opened, I perceived the stench of hell and the breaths of the infernal prison, a single breath of which, were it to blow upon all contingent things, would return them to utter nonexistence. He placed me in the hand of this oppressor who rose against you in rejection and inflicted upon you that which changed the names and their kingdom and severed the attributes from their seats. When I became aware of him and of what I had committed by assisting in what he committed, I fled from him and from meeting him to the court of the glory of your oneness and the seat of the throne of your grandeur.

480 Look upon me, then, my God, with the glances of your care, and turn the gaze of your grace toward this wronged one who, through his own deed, has despaired of your solace and care and lost hope in the wonders of your grace and generosity. Ah, ah, for the greatness of my affliction, the depth of my bewilderment, and my burning anguish! I do not know what to ask of the wonders of your grace, for I see that whatever my highest perception can reach will not calm my soul or bring my heart to rest. Since I find myself in this state and these conditions, I wish to entrust my affair to your hand and into your grasp, so that you may ordain what is best in your sight for my soul, my being, and my reality. I ask you, then, my Beloved, by the manifestations of your Cause in these days, the dawning places of your revelation, and the treasuries of your knowledge, to send down upon me that which will make my face shine amid the heavens and the earth. You have power over whatever you will, and you are the All-Powerful, the All-Subduing, the Almighty, the Self-Subsisting.

481 Now, my Beloved, agitation has seized me once again because of my most grievous sin, you in whose hand are the kingdom of fulfillment and the dominion of decree. Whenever I calm my soul, speak to it of your mercy that preceded all contingent things, remind it of your care that encompasses everyone in earth and heaven, and say to it, “Be at peace and do not despair; my Beloved is merciful, my Sovereign is generous, my Master is tenderhearted, and my Creator is forgiving,” the fire of regret and shame rises from it, consuming my patience and its patience, my endurance and its endurance, my tranquility and its tranquility. Thus my cry will never cease before you, nor will my clamor end before your face. By your might, I fear that my sorrow will grieve the inhabitants of the dominion of your joy and the tribes of the kingdom of your gladness. I ask them, by you, not to prevent me from crying out and lamenting before the city of your unity, for those who circle your throne, have drunk the heavenly waters of glory and hope, and have tasted the sweetness of nearness and reunion should look upon the one who is far from the court of your presence and has been deprived of your wondrous blessings.

482 Ah, ah, my Beloved! Ah, ah, my Goal! Ah, ah, my Desire! Ah, ah, my Hope! Ah, ah! How can I raise my head before the pen of ʿAlī, which you made the dawning place of your revelation and the rising place of your inspiration? How can I look upon the pen associated with Muḥammad, your Messenger, through which the mysteries of your decree were inscribed and the tablets of your Cause were written? By your might, my Beloved, I am on the verge of returning to nonexistence from my shame and from what has befallen me in my days. Can I look upon the pen that set down what you revealed to the Spirit of the ordinances of your Cause and the mysteries of your ways and decree, and from which appeared that which established that your essence is exalted above all likenesses and your self sanctified above all forms? Or can I hear the scratching of the pen of the one who spoke with you, through which he wrote your laws and your wisdom, the gems of your unity and the signs of your uniqueness?

483 O Beloved, how can I look upon the pen of the Friend whom you sent at your command and made the lamp of your revelation among your servants and the word of your command among your creatures? Ah, ah, for my shame, which will never leave me as long as your might endures and your being remains! Suppose, my God, that you immersed me in the oceans of forgiveness: how could the odors of disobedience leave me, when no one but me has committed such disobedience in your days? My God, by your forbearance, through which you have not seized your enemies but have instead sustained them through the sovereignty of your decree, so that whenever anyone sought to draw the sword of hatred against your face, you sharpened it for a wisdom hidden from the eyes of your creatures; and by your care, which neither the affairs of your creation nor the deeds of the idolaters in your lands have withheld, I ask you to return me to nonexistence. Then raise me up once more in your days through the sovereignty of your will, so that what proceeded from me in the first existence may be erased from my heart and I may be unaware of what I committed. Make this unawareness a blessing upon my soul, a mercy upon my being, and a favor to my self. You are indeed the All-Powerful, the Most Exalted, the Almighty, the Generous. End of quotation.

484 By him in whose hand is my soul, if all who are in creation adorned themselves with the garment of fairness, the lament of the pen and the sound of its scratching before the All-Merciful would cause them all to detach themselves from everyone in the heavens and on earth and turn toward the dwelling of the Friend, and to detach themselves from the world and whatever has been ordained within it. Yet in these days such souls are nowhere to be seen, except for very, very few. At what time and hour will radiant, sanctified faces emerge from the bosom of oneness, receive the breaths of God and his words, and become independent of all but him? They are among the choicest of creation in the sight of God, the King, the All-Protecting, the Self-Subsisting.

485 Whatever this servant mentions, he afterward finds himself regretting it, for it is uncertain whether the warmth of divine love will be kindled in those hearts. Thus, in the Revelation of the year sixty, whenever the utterances of the Point of the Bayān—may the souls of all besides him be sacrificed for him—were brought before anyone, he would take them in one hand with utter indifference, look at them for ten minutes or less, and cast them aside. They said, “These words are fabrications.” Had they been even somewhat fair, they would not have exchanged a single letter of them for all who are in the heavens and on earth. Listen to this servant, and purify your heart and hearing of everything you have heard and understood. Then examine the verses revealed before, which descended upon the prophets and messengers, and behold these most wondrous, most exalted verses, and judge fairly for yourself. If you act upon what has been stated, you will assuredly attain the Truth and be freed from the whisperings of devils.

486 The intention was to cite all the revealed verses that have descended from the heaven of the will of the Most High, the Most Exalted Lord concerning this most wondrous, most exalted Cause. He forbade it, saying, “Wait a while, so that it may become clear at what rank and station the people of the Bayān stand, and its essence may be gathered—that is, those souls who have not looked, and do not look, to anyone but God. Then, if it is fitting, the hidden verses of the Bayān and its concealed meanings will descend from the heaven of the will of the All-Merciful, so that some may understand that the Pen of God has moved only to describe this Most Great Revelation, and the Tongue of God has spoken only to mention this Most Pure Beauty.” The matter is in God’s hand, and he has power over all things.

487 This mortal servant has therefore contented himself with those radiant verses that have shone from the dawning place of the mouth of the Sovereign of Utterance.
